## What #545 is, and where the chain stands

#545 de-fatalised five arms of the DC21285 "footbridge" model: a guest write to IRQ_STATUS (+0x180), IRQ_RAW_STATUS
(+0x184), FIQ_STATUS (+0x280) or FIQ_RAW_STATUS (+0x284), or a read of IRQEnableClear (+0x18c), used to end the
emulator.  Now each is DECLINED (no register changes; a declined read returns 0) and ANNOUNCED ONCE per site through
a warn-once latch.  The detector is `regress/diff_footbridge.c`, an offline differential that `#include`s the real
device and calls its access function directly, pinned by `regress/gate_offline.sh`.

Passes so far: pass 1, pass 2 (-> repair 1, 491 rows), repair 1's pass (-> repair 2, the CLASS CLOSURE, 707 rows),
and repair 2's closing pass: panel `panel_20260923_172545`, a dedicated codex job, and the Opus measuring seat, which
built 136 new one-line variants of the five arms and the head predicates and found **28 real changes that pass all
707 rows and are not in the contract's NOT COVERED list** (`_scratchpad/fbd5/q2c/opus/SUMMARY.txt`,
`step3_table.txt`).  **That is the fourth pass in a row to find cells of one space.**  What repair 2 missed was not
a cell of the handler's inputs but **the INSTANCE every row runs on** -- a calloc'd struct with its interrupt fields
seeded and ONE counter pair shared by the CPU interrupt and the four timer interrupts -- and **the PRODUCER's
domains**: the widths the ARM load/store unit emits, the lines a device raises, the fields only the interrupt
producer writes.  **This pass verifies repair 3's enumeration; it is not asked to hunt cells one at a time.**

## What repair 3 changes (the full diff is inlined below)

* **X6/X7/X9/X10: width 8** at +0x180 and +0x280 -- the only two sites an LDRD/STRD can reach (the load/store path
  aligns the address down to 8 before `memory_rw`, so +0x184/+0x18c/+0x284 never see len 8).  X6 +32, X7 +8, X9 +8,
  X10 +8 rows.
* **The return value** is now required (`rv == 1`) on X7 and X10-X13 (it was captured nowhere).
* **X11** runs in all four states (+60 rows) and, after each ordered pair, RE-DECLINES both sites: nothing announced.
* **X13** (20 rows): the legal IRQ Clear write at len 1/2 with 0x70 and len 1/2/4 with all-ones data, with EVERY
  latch set (X12 has the same cells with the latches clear).
* **X14** (19 rows), the instance cells: X14 builds its own instances -- the CPU line counted on its OWN pair,
  optionally DEVINIT's timer reset values, and a device line raised through the REAL `footbridge_interrupt_assert()`:
  (1) the Clear write's drive test at its two missing operand cells (both nonzero and DISJOINT; status zero with the
  enable nonzero); (2) each of the seven lines a device raises (4-7 timers, 10 cats ISA, 11 netwinder ISA, 18 cats PCI)
  pending and enabled ALONE; (3) DEVINIT's timer values, then the same with a tick pending: the five legal reads, a
  Set then a Clear, the five declines; (4) `irq_asserted = 1` through the producer: an IRQ_STATUS read, a Clear 0x10
  (deassert) and 0x400 (assert), and a re-arm row (five declines, the legal reads and a Clear, five declines again --
  five announces in all).  Every X14 row that checks a Clear's drive also requires the SHARED pair unmoved.
* **The CONTRACT** (the comment above X6) gains the producer domains, the instance axis, the new COVERED cells and
  new NOT COVERED lines; `gate_offline.sh` pins the new counts and corrects three of #545's own comments.

## AC �?" SETTLED PREMISES (recorded resolutions; say if the diff or the handler contradicts one)

1. **The product change is settled** -- decline + once-latched announce at the five sites, 0 for the declined read.
   No repair touches `dev_footbridge.c`.
2. **The floor** (the carrier's DEPENDS-ON rule, bounded): the predicates DOMINATING each edited decline, walked to
   the function boundary, crossed with the body-read lvalues' BODY-TESTED partitions where jointly reachable; a value
   or combination the bodies never test as a case is DISCLOSED (clause e), not rowed.  The contract may claim MORE
   than the floor; a claimed cell that a measured edit passes is then a breach of the contract, and repair 3 rows it
   (or narrows the claim) rather than arguing it is above the floor.
3. **Insertion-class edits in OTHER cases** (the +0x188 Set arm, the FIQ enable pair, TIMER_1_VALUE) are outside the
   closure; their existing rows stay, ungraded as floor.  `fbenablewidth` (narrow Set/FIQ writes) is filed.
4. **`fiq_status` has NO writer** in the product: every FIQ status value the rows seed is synthetic.  **`irq_status`
   and `irq_asserted` are written ONLY by `footbridge_interrupt_assert` / `_deassert`** (below).
5. **Width 8 reaches +0x180 and +0x280 only** (the Opus seat, from `cpu_arm_instr_loadstore.c`: `addr &= ~(datalen
   - 1)` before one `memory_rw` of len 8; `memory_rw` passes len through).  An 8-byte access to +0x184, +0x18c or
   +0x284 is not a guest input; the Opus seat's five unaligned width-8 rows were therefore NOT adopted.
6. **The lines a device raises**: DEVINIT connects the four timers to lines 4-7; `bus_pci.c` routes cats ISA to line
   10 (`"%s.10.isa"`), netwinder ISA to 11 (`irq_path_isa` `.11`), and the cats dec21143 to 18 (`irq = 18`).  No
   device raises the other 25 lines, and a guest cannot set a bit of `irq_status`.
7. **DEVINIT's timer reset values**: `timer_control[i] = TIMER_MODE_PERIODIC` (0x40), `timer_load[i] = TIMER_MAX_VAL`
   (0x00FFFFFF), `pending_bound[i] = 1`; a calloc'd instance has all three at 0.

## The handler -- `DEVICE_ACCESS(footbridge)`, the parts this closure is about (verbatim, frozen copy)

NOT SHOWN: the PCI-config, UART and timer arms between `switch (relative_addr) {` and `case IRQ_STATUS:`, and the
timer arms after `case FIQ_ENABLE_CLEAR:`.  They read and write no field the five arms read, EXCEPT that a timer
tick reaches `irq_status` through the interrupt callbacks (see AC.5).

```c
DEVICE_ACCESS(footbridge)
{
	struct footbridge_data *d = (struct footbridge_data *) extra;
	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
	int timer_nr = 0;

	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);

	if (relative_addr >= TIMER_1_LOAD && relative_addr <= TIMER_4_CLEAR) {
		timer_nr = (relative_addr >> 5) & (N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS - 1);
		relative_addr &= ~0x060;
	}

	switch (relative_addr) {
	/* ... NOT SHOWN: VENDOR_ID, DEVICE_ID, PCI config, UART ... */

	case IRQ_STATUS:
		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
			odata = d->irq_status & d->irq_enable;
		else if (!d->warned_irq_status_w) {
			d->warned_irq_status_w = 1;
			fatal("[ WARNING: footbridge write to irq status? "
			    "(once) ]\n");
		}
		break;

	case IRQ_RAW_STATUS:
		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
			odata = d->irq_status;
		else if (!d->warned_irq_raw_w) {
			d->warned_irq_raw_w = 1;
			fatal("[ footbridge write to irq_raw_status (once) ]\n");
		}
		break;

	case IRQ_ENABLE_SET:
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
			d->irq_enable |= idata;
			if (d->irq_status & d->irq_enable)
				INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->irq);
			else
				INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->irq);
		} else {
			odata = d->irq_enable;
		}
		break;

	case IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR:
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
			d->irq_enable &= ~idata;
			if (d->irq_status & d->irq_enable)
				INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->irq);
			else
				INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->irq);
		} else if (!d->warned_irq_clear_r) {
			d->warned_irq_clear_r = 1;
			fatal("[ WARNING: footbridge read from "
			    "ENABLE CLEAR? (once) ]\n");
		}
		break;

	case FIQ_STATUS:
		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
			odata = d->fiq_status & d->fiq_enable;
		else if (!d->warned_fiq_status_w) {
			d->warned_fiq_status_w = 1;
			fatal("[ WARNING: footbridge write to fiq status? "
			    "(once) ]\n");
		}
		break;

	case FIQ_RAW_STATUS:
		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
			odata = d->fiq_status;
		else if (!d->warned_fiq_raw_w) {
			d->warned_fiq_raw_w = 1;
			fatal("[ footbridge write to fiq_raw_status (once) ]\n");
		}
		break;

	case FIQ_ENABLE_SET:
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
			d->fiq_enable |= idata;
		break;

	case FIQ_ENABLE_CLEAR:
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
			d->fiq_enable &= ~idata;
		break;

	/* ... NOT SHOWN: the timer arms ... */

	default:if (writeflag == MEM_READ) {
			fatal("[ footbridge: read from 0x%x ]\n",
			    (int)relative_addr);
		} else {
			fatal("[ footbridge: write to 0x%x: 0x%llx ]\n",
			    (int)relative_addr, (long long)idata);
		}
	}

	if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
		memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len, odata);

	return 1;
}
```
(The `#545:` and `#539:` comments inside the arms are omitted here; nothing else is.)  Offsets: IRQ_STATUS 0x180,
IRQ_RAW_STATUS 0x184, IRQ_ENABLE_SET 0x188, IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR 0x18c, FIQ_STATUS 0x280, FIQ_RAW_STATUS 0x284,
FIQ_ENABLE_SET 0x288, FIQ_ENABLE_CLEAR 0x28c; the timer range is TIMER_1_LOAD 0x300 .. TIMER_4_CLEAR 0x36c.


### The producer -- the only writers of `irq_status` and `irq_asserted` (verbatim, frozen `dev_footbridge.c`)

```c
void footbridge_interrupt_assert(struct interrupt *interrupt)
{
	struct footbridge_data *d = (struct footbridge_data *) interrupt->extra;
	d->irq_status |= interrupt->line;

	// Patch from NetBSD:
	// "fix hang interrupt issue. Always assert the irq's even if we have
	//  already asserted the global irq for the device. prevents hangs with
	//  cats."
	if ((d->irq_status & d->irq_enable) /* && !d->irq_asserted */) {
		d->irq_asserted = 1;
		INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->irq);
	}
}

void footbridge_interrupt_deassert(struct interrupt *interrupt)
{
	struct footbridge_data *d = (struct footbridge_data *) interrupt->extra;
	d->irq_status &= ~interrupt->line;

	if (!(d->irq_status & d->irq_enable) && d->irq_asserted) {
		d->irq_asserted = 0;
		INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->irq);
	}
}
```

+/*  #545 repair 3, X14: every other block's instance is a calloc with its interrupt fields seeded, counting calls
+    on the CPU line d->irq and the four timer interrupts on ONE pair (noop_assert/noop_deassert).  X14 builds its
+    instances here instead: the CPU line on its OWN pair, optionally DEVINIT's timer reset values, and a device
+    line raised through the real footbridge_interrupt_assert() -- the product's only writer of irq_status and of
+    irq_asserted.  (The Opus seat's scratch closure experiment, q2c pass; adapted.)  */
+static int cpu_asserts, cpu_deasserts;
+static void cpu_assert(struct interrupt *i) { (void) i; cpu_asserts++; }
+static void cpu_deassert(struct interrupt *i) { (void) i; cpu_deasserts++; }
+static struct footbridge_data *x14_new(uint32_t is, uint32_t ie, uint32_t fs, uint32_t fe, int devinit)
+{
+	struct footbridge_data *x = calloc(1, sizeof(*x));
+	int i;
+	arm_irqs(x);
+	x->irq.interrupt_assert = cpu_assert;
+	x->irq.interrupt_deassert = cpu_deassert;
+	x->irq_status = is; x->irq_enable = ie; x->fiq_status = fs; x->fiq_enable = fe;
+	if (devinit)
+		for (i = 0; i < N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS; i++) {
+			x->timer_control[i] = TIMER_MODE_PERIODIC;
+			x->timer_load[i] = TIMER_MAX_VAL;
+			x->pending_bound[i] = 1;
+		}
+	return x;
+}
+static void x14_line(struct footbridge_data *x, int n)
+{
+	struct interrupt it;
+	memset(&it, 0, sizeof(it));
+	it.line = (uint32_t) 1 << n;
+	it.extra = x;
+	footbridge_interrupt_assert(&it);
+}

+	 *    11 on netwinder, PCI 18 on cats);

+			snprintf(zn, sizeof(zn), "X14 Clear 0, only line %d pending and enabled: CPU line asserted once",
+			    LINE[k]);
+			check_u(zn, rv == 1 && x->irq_enable == (uint32_t) 1 << LINE[k] && ZCPU(1, 0) && fatal_calls == 0, 1);
+			free(x);
+		}
+		/*  DEVINIT's timer reset values, then with a tick pending: the five legal reads return their values and
+		    announce nothing; a Set then a Clear land; the five declines announce exactly 5 and keep the fields.  */
+		for (tk = 0; tk < 2; tk++) {
+			x = x14_new(0x18, 0x30, 0x60, 0xc0, 1);
+			x->pending_timer_interrupts[0] = tk;
+			ZZERO();
+			ok = 1;
+			for (i = 0; i < 5; i++) {
+				memset(b, 0xa5, sizeof(b));
+				rv = dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, RR[i].addr, b, 4, MEM_READ, x);
+				ok = ok && rv == 1 && memory_readmax64(cpu, b, 4) == RR[i].want;
+			}
+			snprintf(zn, sizeof(zn), "X14 %s: the five legal reads return their values", TS[tk]);
+			check_u(zn, ok && fatal_calls == 0, 1);
+			memory_writemax64(cpu, b, 4, 0x40);
+			rv = dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, IRQ_ENABLE_SET, b, 4, MEM_WRITE, x);
+			ok = rv == 1;
+			ZCLR(0x10);
+			snprintf(zn, sizeof(zn), "X14 %s: Set 0x40 then Clear 0x10 leave 0x60", TS[tk]);
+			check_u(zn, ok && rv == 1 && x->irq_enable == 0x60 && fatal_calls == 0, 1);
+			ok = 1;
+			ZDECLINE();
+			snprintf(zn, sizeof(zn), "X14 %s: the five declines announce 5, keep the fields", TS[tk]);
+			check_u(zn, ok && fatal_calls == 5 && x->irq_status == 0x18 && x->irq_enable == 0x60
+			    && x->fiq_status == 0x60 && x->fiq_enable == 0xc0, 1);
+			free(x);
+		}
+		/*  irq_asserted = 1, through the real callback: a device raises line 4 with line 4 enabled.  */
+		x = x14_new(0, 0x10, 0, 0, 0);
+		ZZERO();
+		x14_line(x, 4);
+		ok = x->irq_asserted == 1 && x->irq_status == 0x10 && ZCPU(1, 0);
+		ZZERO();
+		memset(b, 0xa5, sizeof(b));
+		rv = dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, IRQ_STATUS, b, 4, MEM_READ, x);
+		check_u("X14 line 4 raised by a device: IRQ_STATUS reads 0x10",
+		    ok && rv == 1 && memory_readmax64(cpu, b, 4) == 0x10 && fatal_calls == 0, 1);
+		free(x);
+		for (k = 0; k < 2; k++) {
+			x = x14_new(0, 0x10, 0, 0, 0);
+			x14_line(x, 4);
+			ok = x->irq_asserted == 1;
+			ZZERO();
+			ZCLR(k ? 0x400 : 0x10);
+			snprintf(zn, sizeof(zn), "X14 line 4 raised by a device: Clear %s, CPU line %s once",
+			    k ? "0x400 keeps it" : "0x10 clears it", k ? "asserted" : "deasserted");
+			check_u(zn, ok && rv == 1 && x->irq_enable == (k ? 0x10u : 0u) && (k ? ZCPU(1, 0) : ZCPU(0, 1))
+			    && fatal_calls == 0, 1);
+			free(x);
+		}
+		x = x14_new(0, 0x10, 0, 0, 0);
+		x14_line(x, 4);
+		ok = x->irq_asserted == 1;
+		ZZERO();
+		ZDECLINE();
+		f0 = fatal_calls;
+		for (i = 0; i < 5; i++) {
+			rv = dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, RR[i].addr, b, 4, MEM_READ, x);
+			ok = ok && rv == 1;
+		}
+		ZCLR(0x400);
+		ok = ok && rv == 1;
+		ZDECLINE();
+		check_u("X14 line 4 raised by a device: 5 declines, then the legal reads and a Clear re-arm no latch",
+		    ok && f0 == 5 && fatal_calls == 5, 1);
+		free(x);
+#undef ZDECLINE
+#undef ZCLR
+#undef ZCPU
+#undef ZZERO
+		fatal_calls = 0;
+	}

## AF2 �?" the closing pass's findings, with MY disposition of each

*(Self-review disclosed: I wrote repairs 1-3 and every disposition below.  Seats whose finding I adopted: check that
the ROWS answer your finding, not merely that the finding was right.  The closing pass's verdicts on repair 2: codex,
grok, glm, deepseek, minimax and the kimi substitute DO NOT SHIP; agy split; the kimi CLI walled; the Opus seat
DO NOT SHIP by measurement.)*

**G1 -- width 8** (grok; my S8/S9/S10).  An LDRD/STRD at +0x180 or +0x280 is ONE 8-byte access; no row used len 8.
**ACCEPTED** -> X6/X7/X9/X10 width-8 cells at those two sites.  S8 7 red, S9 8, S10 7.
**G2 -- the handler's return value was never read** (grok S11; codex-dedicated).  **ACCEPTED** -> `rv == 1` on X7 and
X10-X13.  S11 (`if (len != 4) return 0;` after the Clear write's drive) 32 red.
**G3 -- no narrow Clear write with its latch set; no latched all-ones Clear** (codex-dedicated S12).  **ACCEPTED** ->
X13.  S12 (`writeflag == MEM_WRITE && (len == 4 || !d->warned_irq_clear_r)`) 16 red.
**G4 -- a decline clearing ANOTHER site's latch** (codex; S13 `warned_irq_raw_w = 0` in the IRQ_STATUS decline, S14
the FIQ_RAW mirror).  X11 never re-declined the earlier site.  **ACCEPTED** -> X11 re-declines both sites after each
pair.  S13, S14 red.
**G5 -- a field x other-latch joint outside the seeded state** (glm S6: `!(d->irq_status == 0 && d->warned_irq_raw_w)`;
X11 ran seeded only).  **ACCEPTED** -> X11 in all four states.  S6 red.
**G6 -- narrow Set / FIQ-enable writes** (deepseek, kimi substitute, minimax: S1-S3) **and the fetch exempting only the
Clear address** (minimax S4).  **DISCLOSED, outside the floor**: those arms share no case with a decline, and the
fetch feeds only `idata`, which no decline reads.  Filed `fbenablewidth`.  The contract's "legal x width" sentence was
a wrong record (L4) and now names them.
**G7 -- exact DATA values** (the Opus seat's `idata != 0x20`, S7) **and a narrow ZERO-data or latched zero-data Clear
write** (codex-dedicated: no row makes either).  **DISCLOSED** (clause e): the Clear body computes with `idata`, it
never tests it as a case; the contract's NOT COVERED names exact field OR DATA values and the joints.
**G8 -- the read tail `if (writeflag == MEM_READ) memory_writemax64(...)`, missing from THE SPACE** (codex-dedicated).
**NO ROW OWED**: it post-dominates rather than dominates the declines, and X6's "a declined read returns 0 over its
width" already fails if the tail skips a read.
**G9 -- three false comments in #545's own gate section** (codex-dedicated: "every grep below fails on a truncated
log"; "forked so a re-crash is a named-row KILL" -- already measured false by the section's own ON THE FORKS
paragraph; "every legal access with every latch set").  **ACCEPTED**, all three corrected.

interrupt calls 5, the timer fields and irq_asserted 6, the fetch 3, width 8 3, a latch re-arm 1).  **ACCEPTED** -> X14, plus

## OPERATIONAL RULE FOR EVERY SEAT (appended by panel.sh, not by the author)

**Do not run `run.sh`, any `gate_*.sh`, or the battery.** Every measurement you need is
inlined above. A gate takes minutes -- longer than your response budget -- and a second
concurrent invocation corrupts the operator run in progress, because the gates share one
log directory. A seat that runs a gate produces no review AND may invalidate someone
else s. Building and running a single detector or mutant in your own temp directory is
fine and encouraged; driving the harness is not.

If you want a measurement that is not inlined, NAME IT and say why. Naming it is worth
far more to this panel than a timeout.

  24: ## What #545 is, and where the chain stands
  25: 
  26: #545 de-fatalised five arms of the DC21285 "footbridge" model: a guest write to IRQ_STATUS (+0x180), IRQ_RAW_STATUS
  27: (+0x184), FIQ_STATUS (+0x280) or FIQ_RAW_STATUS (+0x284), or a read of IRQEnableClear (+0x18c), used to end the
  28: emulator.  Now each is DECLINED (no register changes; a declined read returns 0) and ANNOUNCED ONCE per site through
  29: a warn-once latch.  The detector is `regress/diff_footbridge.c`, an offline differential that `#include`s the real
  30: device and calls its access function directly, pinned by `regress/gate_offline.sh`.
  31: 
  32: Passes so far: pass 1, pass 2 (-> repair 1, 491 rows), repair 1's pass (-> repair 2, the CLASS CLOSURE, 707 rows),
  33: and repair 2's closing pass: panel `panel_20260923_172545`, a dedicated codex job, and the Opus measuring seat, which
  34: built 136 new one-line variants of the five arms and the head predicates and found **28 real changes that pass all
  35: 707 rows and are not in the contract's NOT COVERED list** (`_scratchpad/fbd5/q2c/opus/SUMMARY.txt`,
  36: `step3_table.txt`).  **That is the fourth pass in a row to find cells of one space.**  What repair 2 missed was not
  37: a cell of the handler's inputs but **the INSTANCE every row runs on** -- a calloc'd struct with its interrupt fields
  38: seeded and ONE counter pair shared by the CPU interrupt and the four timer interrupts -- and **the PRODUCER's
  39: domains**: the widths the ARM load/store unit emits, the lines a device raises, the fields only the interrupt

  86:    the function boundary, crossed with the body-read lvalues' BODY-TESTED partitions where jointly reachable; a value
  87:    or combination the bodies never test as a case is DISCLOSED (clause e), not rowed.  The contract may claim MORE
  88:    than the floor; a claimed cell that a measured edit passes is then a breach of the contract, and repair 3 rows it

  96:    +0x284 is not a guest input; the Opus seat's five unaligned width-8 rows were therefore NOT adopted.

  98:    10 (`"%s.10.isa"`), netwinder ISA to 11 (`irq_path_isa` `.11`), and the cats dec21143 to 18 (`irq = 18`).  No
  99:    device raises the other 25 lines, and a guest cannot set a bit of `irq_status`.
 100: 7. **DEVINIT's timer reset values**: `timer_control[i] = TIMER_MODE_PERIODIC` (0x40), `timer_load[i] = TIMER_MAX_VAL`

 103: ## The handler -- `DEVICE_ACCESS(footbridge)`, the parts this closure is about (verbatim, frozen copy)
 104: 
 105: NOT SHOWN: the PCI-config, UART and timer arms between `switch (relative_addr) {` and `case IRQ_STATUS:`, and the
 106: timer arms after `case FIQ_ENABLE_CLEAR:`.  They read and write no field the five arms read, EXCEPT that a timer
 107: tick reaches `irq_status` through the interrupt callbacks (see C.5).
 108: 
 109: ```c

 142: 			fatal("[ footbridge write to irq_raw_status (once) ]\n");
 143: 		}
 144: 		break;
 145: 
 146: 	case IRQ_ENABLE_SET:
 147: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
 148: 			d->irq_enable |= idata;

 170: 		break;

 176: 			d->warned_fiq_status_w = 1;
 177: 			fatal("[ WARNING: footbridge write to fiq status? "

 183: 		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)

 216: }

 223: ### The producer -- the only writers of `irq_status` and `irq_asserted` (verbatim, frozen `dev_footbridge.c`)
 224: 
 225: ```c
 226: void footbridge_interrupt_assert(struct interrupt *interrupt)
 227: {
 228: 	struct footbridge_data *d = (struct footbridge_data *) interrupt->extra;
 229: 	d->irq_status |= interrupt->line;
 230: 
 231: 	// Patch from NetBSD:
 232: 	// "fix hang interrupt issue. Always assert the irq's even if we have
 233: 	//  already asserted the global irq for the device. prevents hangs with
 234: 	//  cats."
 235: 	if ((d->irq_status & d->irq_enable) /* && !d->irq_asserted */) {
 236: 		d->irq_asserted = 1;
 237: 		INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->irq);
 238: 	}
 239: }
 240: 
 241: void footbridge_interrupt_deassert(struct interrupt *interrupt)
 242: {
 243: 	struct footbridge_data *d = (struct footbridge_data *) interrupt->extra;
 244: 	d->irq_status &= ~interrupt->line;
 245: 
 246: 	if (!(d->irq_status & d->irq_enable) && d->irq_asserted) {
 247: 		d->irq_asserted = 0;
 248: 		INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->irq);
 249: 	}
 250: }
 251: ```

  60: struct footbridge_data {
  61: 	struct interrupt irq;
  62: 
  63: 	struct pci_data *pcibus;
  64: 
  65: 	int		console_handle;
  66: 
  67: 	uint32_t	timer_load[N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS];
  68: 	uint32_t	timer_value[N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS];
  69: 	uint32_t	timer_control[N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS];
  70: 
  71: 	struct interrupt timer_irq[N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS];
  72: 	struct timer	*timer[N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS];
  73: 	int		pending_timer_interrupts[N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS];
  74: 	/*  #442: the per-timer bound on the counter above, one emulated second of
  75: 	    that timer's delivered rate, recomputed by reload_timer_value().  */
  76: 	int		pending_bound[N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS];
  77: 
  78: 	int		irq_asserted;
  79: 
  80: 	uint32_t	irq_status;
  81: 	uint32_t	irq_enable;
  82:

  84: 	uint32_t	fiq_enable;
  85: 
  86: 	/*  #545: one warn-once latch per undefined-direction access that used
  87: 	    to end the process (DC21285 7.3.29-7.3.33: "The Status and RawStatus
  88: 	    are read-only, and EnableClear and Soft are write-only").  Zeroed by
  89: 	    DEVINIT's memset and by the offline differentials' calloc.  */
  90: 	int		warned_irq_status_w;
  91: 	int		warned_irq_raw_w;
  92: 	int		warned_irq_clear_r;
  93: 	int		warned_fiq_status_w;
  94: 	int		warned_fiq_raw_w;
  95: };
  96: 
  97: 
  98: /*
  99:  *  #442: the counter above was incremented without a bound, so a guest that armed
 100:  *  a timer and never acked overflowed it -- and on overflow the int goes negative,
 101:  *  DEVICE_TICK's `> 0` is false, and THE EMULATED CLOCK DELIVERS NOTHING FOR 2^31
 102:  *  FURTHER TICKS.  Reproduced at rung 3 on an unmodified -E cats: two MMIO writes,
 103:  *  then 42.95 s to the wrap and a 40 s dead window, measured three times.
 104:  *
 105:  *  THE COMPARISON PRECEDES THE INCREMENT, and that is not a style choice.  An
 106:  *  increment-then-check form -- which is what the one in-tree precedent for this
 107:  *  (dev_luna88k.c:252) actually does -- reintroduces the UB it is meant to remove
 108:  *  if the counter ever reaches INT_MAX, because the ++ has already executed by the
 109:  *  time the check runs.  luna88k is safe only because its own reset keeps the
 110:  *  counter far below INT_MAX; the FORM is still the wrong one to copy.
 111:  *
 112:  *  Reset to 1 rather than 0, so one interrupt is still owed -- coalescing the debt
 113:  *  rather than discarding it.  MEASURED to matter: a reset-to-0 mutant survives the
 114:  *  rung-3 drain probe and two of the three offline rows; only the trough row
 115:  *  separates them (0 against 1).
 116:  *
 117:  *  NO MESSAGE FROM HERE.  These run in the SIGALRM handler and debugmsg() is
 118:  *  printf underneath, so copying luna88k's diagnostic would put four printf paths
 119:  *  inside a signal handler -- and timer.c:274-279 states that nothing in that file
 120:  *  prints from the handler.  (luna88k's own callback does, which is a defect there;
 121:  *  filed, not fixed here.)  If a diagnostic is ever wanted, latch a flag here and
 122:  *  report from DEVICE_TICK, which runs in the main loop.
 123:  */
 124: static void footbridge_pending(struct footbridge_data *d, int nr)
 125: {
 126: 	if (d->pending_timer_interrupts[nr] >= d->pending_bound[nr])
 127: 		d->pending_timer_interrupts[nr] = 1;
 128: 	else
 129: 		d->pending_timer_interrupts[nr] ++;
 130: }
 131: 
 132: static void timer_tick0(struct timer *t, void *extra)
 133: { footbridge_pending((struct footbridge_data *)extra, 0); }
 134: static void timer_tick1(struct timer *t, void *extra)
 135: { footbridge_pending((struct footbridge_data *)extra, 1); }
 136: static void timer_tick2(struct timer *t, void *extra)
 137: { footbridge_pending((struct footbridge_data *)extra, 2); }
 138: static void timer_tick3(struct timer *t, void *extra)
 139: { footbridge_pending((struct footbridge_data *)extra, 3); }
 140: 
 141: 
 142: /*
 143:  *  #432: ONE interpretation of the load register, used by BOTH consumers.
 144:  *
 145:  *  THE CRASH.  DEVICE_TICK computed `random() % timer_load[i]`, and TIMER_x_LOAD accepts a
 146:  *  guest-written zero (`idata & TIMER_MAX_VAL`), so a guest could divide by zero and take the
 147:  *  HOST process down -- reproduced as SIGFPE, exit 136, by two seats independently.
 148:  *
 149:  *  THERE ARE TWO ROUTES TO IT, and only one is ours.  Route 1: a zero load makes the rate
 150:  *  +INF, which before #427/#428 meant interval 0.0 and a catch-up loop that never advanced,
 151:  *  so the machine froze before this function ran; the clamp and the bound opened that path.
 152:  *  ROUTE 2 PREDATES BOTH: TIMER_x_LOAD does not clear pending_timer_interrupts (TIMER_x_CONTROL
 153:  *  below does), so an ORDINARY LEGAL timer accrues a backlog and a later write of zero divides
 154:  *  -- measured on the pre-#427 core, it crashes there too.  DEVICE_TICK runs from machine_run(),
 155:  *  the main loop, not from the signal handler, so the CPU reaches the divide between signals.
 156:  *
 157:  *  WHY A HELPER RATHER THAN TWO GUARDS.  Three seats converged on this independently, and the
 158:  *  measuring seat REFUTED each single-site alternative: guarding only this function still
 159:  *  SIGFPEs by route 2 (and is the most dangerous option precisely because it passes the obvious
 160:  *  test); guarding only the modulo leaves the backlog growing 1048576 per signal, reaching
 161:  *  INT_MAX in ~31 s; and normalising at the guest write corrupts read-back -- TIMER_x_LOAD
 162:  *  returns the stored value, so the guest would read something it never wrote, which invents an
 163:  *  answer to a question the hardware documentation we lack would have to settle.
 164:  *
 165:  *  THE REGISTER STAYS RAW.  timer_load[] keeps exactly what the guest wrote, masked to 24 bits.
 166:  *  Only the DERIVED quantities are interpreted here, and they are model-internal.
 167:  *
 168:  *  ZERO MEANS FULL PERIOD (2^24), and it is a CHOICE UNDER A DOCUMENTED UNKNOWN, not a fact.
 169:  *
 170:  *  The 21285 datasheet was obtained for this round (_scratchpad/dc21285.pdf, Intel order
 171:  *  278115-001, Sept 1998).  SS 7.3.39 (p. 7-50) specifies TimerNLoad and its reset value without
 172:  *  saying what a count of zero does, and a search of the full extraction for "minimum count" /
 173:  *  "nonzero" / "reaches zero" turns up no equivalent of the 8254's Figure 22.  So the absence is
 174:  *  MEASURED, not assumed -- a stronger thing to say than the guess it replaces.
 175:  *
 176:  *  BUT "SILENT" OVERSTATES IT BY HALF, and the pass-2 review caught that in this round's favour.
 177:  *  SS 7.3.41 bit 6 says a FREE-RUNNING timer "will wrap to FFFFFFh and continue to decrement",
 178:  *  which together with SS 6.4's 24-bit down counter DETERMINES the free-run case: a load of zero
 179:  *  gives a period of exactly 2^24, i.e. the mapping below is datasheet-IMPLIED there -- and
 180:  *  free-running is bit 6 == 0, the reset mode.  It is genuinely unknown only for PERIODIC.
 181:  *
 182:  *  The choice therefore rests on failure asymmetry, and there are THREE candidate truths rather
 183:  *  than the two first written here.  Implement full-period when the truth is "disabled" and a
 184:  *  guest gets a few unwanted interrupts on a timer it explicitly enabled -- noisy, visible,
 185:  *  recoverable.  Implement "disabled" when the truth is full-period and the guest waits forever
 186:  *  on a tick that never comes -- silent, and it hangs at boot.  And the third, which the first
 187:  *  draft omitted: read mechanically, PERIODIC mode with a zero load reloads zero and interrupts
 188:  *  every prescaled clock -- a max-rate storm, the DANGEROUS branch rather than the silent one.
 189:  *  Full-period is the only choice that survives all three, so the argument was understating its
 190:  *  own robustness.
 191:  *
 192:  *  A SECOND SUPPORT WAS ALSO WITHDRAWN, for the same reason as the third and by a seat whose
 193:  *  answer sat unread for a day.  It said the 24-bit mask aliases "a legitimate guest write of
 194:  *  0x1000000 -- one full wrap -- to zero, so this mapping keeps the rate continuous across the
 195:  *  register's own truncation".  The datasheet closes it: SS 7.3.39 gives TimerNLoad as bits 23:0
 196:  *  R/W with "31:24 -- R Read only as 0", so bit 24 is not a truncated write at all, it is OUTSIDE
 197:  *  THE WRITABLE FIELD.  There is no wrap to be continuous across, and continuity beyond a
 198:  *  register's own width is a modelling preference dressed up as arithmetic.  Zero -> 2^24 stands
 199:  *  on the three-way argument above and on nothing else; what silicon does with a zero load
 200:  *  remains UNKNOWN, and SS 7.3.39 is silent on it.
 201:  *
 202:  *  A THIRD ARGUMENT WAS WITHDRAWN WHEN THE DATASHEET ARRIVED, and it is recorded because the
 203:  *  withdrawal matters more than the argument did.  An earlier draft of this comment said
 204:  *  "devinit resets every load to TIMER_MAX_VAL, so the model's own convention is full width".
 205:  *  The datasheet says TimerNLoad and TimerNControl BOTH reset to 0 (SS 7.3.39, SS 7.3.41), so
 206:  *  this model's reset values are themselves wrong -- that line was citing a model bug as
 207:  *  evidence.  The divergence is filed; it is not fixed here.
 208:  *
 209:  *  THE WIDTH FIX IS NOT SEPARABLE FROM THE ZERO FIX.  The old `int cycles` with `cycles <<= 8`
 210:  *  overflowed for exactly half the legal 24-bit range (first bad load 0x800000), yielding a
 211:  *  NEGATIVE frequency that the core's floor clamp silently turned into one tick per ~3.2 years
 212:  *  -- reachable with NO load write at all, since the reset load is TIMER_MAX_VAL and a single
 213:  *  write of ENABLE|FCLK_256 gets there.  And mapping zero to 2^24 while leaving `int` gives
 214:  *  2^24 << 8 == 2^32 == 0, which is +INF AGAIN: the obvious zero-guard RE-CREATES the bug it
 215:  *  removes.  Hence uint64_t, and multiplication rather than a signed shift.  Found independently
 216:  *  by three seats.
 217:  *
 218:  *  (8253 maps a zero divisor to "disabled" instead -- and a pass-2 seat caught that an earlier
 219:  *  version of this parenthetical argued the point BACKWARDS.  It said the 8253's 16-bit counter
 220:  *  "has no equivalent of the 24-bit aliasing above", but writing 0x10000 to a 16-bit latch aliases
 221:  *  to zero in exactly the same way -- which is precisely WHY the Intel 8254 datasheet defines a
 222:  *  programmed count of 0 as 2^16 (231164-005, p. 17).  So the aliasing argument REINFORCES this
 223:  *  mapping rather than distinguishing it, and dev_8253.c's "disabled" is the one that looks wrong
 224:  *  against its own hardware.  Filed as its own item; not changed here.)
 225:  */
 226: /*
 227:  *  with_prescaler distinguishes the two consumers, and it is NOT a convenience flag -- getting
 228:  *  it wrong is a defect the measuring seat caught in its own first proposal.  The prescaler
 229:  *  slows the RATE; it does not widen the COUNTER.  Feeding the prescaled period to the modulo
 230:  *  put values up to 0x7fffca11 into a 24-bit register that the guest can read back -- measured,
 231:  *  19833 of 20000 ticks out of range.  So: the rate consumer prescales, the counter consumer
 232:  *  does not, and both get their never-zero span from this one function.
 233:  */
 234: static uint64_t footbridge_effective_cycles(uint32_t load, uint32_t control,
 235: 	int with_prescaler)
 236: {
 237: 	uint64_t cycles = load;
 238: 
 239: 	if (cycles == 0)
 240: 		cycles = (uint64_t)TIMER_MAX_VAL + 1;	/*  0x1000000 == 2^24  */
 241: 
 242: 	if (!with_prescaler)
 243: 		return cycles;
 244: 
 245: 	/*  The prescaler scales the PERIOD.  It must not be a signed shift, and the order
 246: 	    matters: TIMER_EXTERNAL is FCLK_16|FCLK_256, so test the pair first or 0x0C reads
 247: 	    as a bare FCLK_16 by luck of the `else if`.  */
 248: 	if ((control & (TIMER_FCLK_16 | TIMER_FCLK_256))
 249: 	    == (TIMER_FCLK_16 | TIMER_FCLK_256)) {
 250: 		/*  TIMER_EXTERNAL (0x0C): a LEGAL encoding, and this is now DATASHEET-BACKED
 251: 		    rather than inferred from the header.  The 21285 datasheet obtained for this
 252: 		    round gives TimerNControl bits 3:2 as 00=fclk_in, 01=fclk_in/16,
 253: 		    10=fclk_in/256, 11=External event (SS 7.3.41, p. 7-51; SS 6.4, p. 6-11 lists
 254: 		    the same four).  It used to reach fatal()+exit(1) below, so a CONFORMING guest
 255: 		    selecting the external clock source killed the host -- the same defect class as
 256: 		    the divide and arguably likelier, being a documented mode rather than a
 257: 		    malformed value.  The RATE an external source would give depends on a pin this
 258: 		    model has no signal for (irq_in_l), so it is genuinely not derivable here and
 259: 		    is NOT invented: the unprescaled period keeps the timer sane and the emulator
 260: 		    alive.  Modelling the external event properly is filed.  */
 261: 		;
 262: 	} else if (control & TIMER_FCLK_16)
 263: 		cycles *= 16;
 264: 	else if (control & TIMER_FCLK_256)
 265: 		cycles *= 256;
 266: 
 267: 	return cycles;
 268: }
 269: 
 270: 
 271: static void reload_timer_value(struct cpu *cpu, struct footbridge_data *d,
 272: 	int timer_nr)
 273: {
 274: 	double freq = (double)cpu->machine->emulated_hz;
 275: 
 276: 	freq /= (double) footbridge_effective_cycles(d->timer_load[timer_nr],
 277: 	    d->timer_control[timer_nr], 1);
 278: 
 279: 	d->timer_value[timer_nr] = d->timer_load[timer_nr];
 280: 
 281: 	/*
 282: 	 *  #442: one emulated second of THIS timer's delivered rate, clamped.
 283: 	 *
 284: 	 *  The ceiling is DERIVED, not chosen: TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP ticks per timer
 285: 	 *  per signal at TIMER_BASE_FREQUENCY signals per second is the most the
 286: 	 *  timer core can ever deliver to one timer in one wall second, so a cap
 287: 	 *  at that value can never throttle a legitimately fast timer.
 288: 	 *
 289: 	 *  IT IS ALSO NOT OPTIONAL, which a purely rate-relative bound hides.
 290: 	 *  MEASURED: with `-I 2147483647` and load 1 the requested rate is
 291: 	 *  2147483647.0, so (int)freq is INT_MAX and a bound of "the timer's own
 292: 	 *  rate" never fires -- the overflow returns, reachable through a
 293: 	 *  documented command-line flag.  Deleting these two lines is the
 294: 	 *  smallest edit that still passes the rung-3 probe.
 295: 	 *
 296: 	 *  Floor 1.  *** ITS ORIGINAL JUSTIFICATION WAS MEASURED FALSE and is
 297: 	 *  corrected here rather than quietly dropped: *** the comment claimed a
 298: 	 *  0.0116 Hz timer "would otherwise bound at 0 and the counter would be
 299: 	 *  pinned there".  With the floor PRESENT that case already reads peak 1,
 300: 	 *  and with the floor DELETED the harness output is BYTE-IDENTICAL -- a
 301: 	 *  bound of 0 and a bound of 1 both pin at 1.  The floor prevents nothing
 302: 	 *  today; it is kept as a guard against a future bound expression that
 303: 	 *  could go negative, and that is the honest reason.
 304: 	 *
 305: 	 *  The comparisons are negated, but NOT for the reason first written.
 306: 	 *  timer_clamp_freq() puts its FLOOR first deliberately (timer.c:80-82:
 307: 	 *  "the FLOOR is tested FIRST so a NaN lands SLOW rather than fast"), and
 308: 	 *  this tests the CEILING first -- the inverse.  Harmless, because NaN is
 309: 	 *  UNREACHABLE here: emulated_hz is an int (machine.h:191) and
 310: 	 *  footbridge_effective_cycles() is >= 1 by construction, so this is
 311: 	 *  finite / finite-nonzero.  The claim to "match timer_clamp_freq's idiom"
 312: 	 *  was false in the one respect that idiom is about.
 313: 	 *
 314: 	 *  Set before timer_add()/timer_update_frequency() below.  *** THE REASON
 315: 	 *  FIRST GIVEN HERE -- that a SIGALRM landing between them would find a
 316: 	 *  zero bound -- IS NOT LOAD-BEARING, measured. ***  On the FIRST arm the
 317: 	 *  timer is not in first_timer until timer_add() links it, and timer.c
 318: 	 *  publishes fully-initialised (next stored before first_timer); on later
 319: 	 *  arms the OLD bound is still a valid bound.  A mutant moving this store
 320: 	 *  below the switch is a ZERO-BYTE object delta and passes every row.
 321: 	 *
 322: 	 *  Kept in this order because it reads correctly and costs nothing, not
 323: 	 *  because it closes a window.  Note also that this and the DEVINIT seed
 324: 	 *  are REDUNDANT WITH EACH OTHER -- either alone removes the zero-bound
 325: 	 *  state -- and neither comment used to mention the other doing the work.
 326: 	 */
 327: 	{
 328: 		double bound = freq;
 329: 		if (!(bound <= (double)TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP * TIMER_BASE_FREQUENCY))
 330: 			bound = (double)TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP * TIMER_BASE_FREQUENCY;
 331: 		if (!(bound >= 1.0))
 332: 			bound = 1.0;
 333: 		d->pending_bound[timer_nr] = (int) bound;
 334: 	}
 335: 
 336: 	/*  printf("%i: %i -> %f Hz\n", timer_nr,
 337: 	    d->timer_load[timer_nr], freq);  */
 338: 
 339: 	if (d->timer[timer_nr] == NULL) {
 340: 		switch (timer_nr) {
 341: 		case 0:	d->timer[0] = timer_add(freq, timer_tick0, d); break;
 342: 		case 1:	d->timer[1] = timer_add(freq, timer_tick1, d); break;
 343: 		case 2:	d->timer[2] = timer_add(freq, timer_tick2, d); break;
 344: 		case 3:	d->timer[3] = timer_add(freq, timer_tick3, d); break;
 345: 		}
 346: 	} else {
 347: 		timer_update_frequency(d->timer[timer_nr], freq);
 348: 	}
 349: }
 350: 
 351: 
 352: /*
 353:  *  The 4 footbridge timers should decrease and cause interrupts. Periodic
 354:  *  interrupts restart as soon as they are acknowledged, non-periodic
 355:  *  interrupts need to be "reloaded" to restart.
 356:  *
 357:  *  TODO: Hm. I thought I had solved this, but it didn't quite work.
 358:  *        This needs to be re-checked against documentation, sometime.
 359:  */
 360: DEVICE_TICK(footbridge)
 361: {
 362: 	struct footbridge_data *d = (struct footbridge_data *) extra;
 363: 	int i;
 364: 
 365: 	for (i=0; i<N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS; i++) {
 366: 		if (d->timer_control[i] & TIMER_ENABLE) {
 367: 			if (d->pending_timer_interrupts[i] > 0) {
 368: 				/*  #432: the same never-zero span, WITHOUT the
 369: 				    prescaler -- this is a 24-bit counter, and the
 370: 				    prescaler slows the rate rather than widening
 371: 				    it.  */
 372: 				d->timer_value[i] = random() %
 373: 				    footbridge_effective_cycles(d->timer_load[i],
 374: 				    d->timer_control[i], 0);
 375: 				INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->timer_irq[i]);
 376: 			}
 377: 		}
 378: 	}
 379: }

 382: /*
 383:  *  footbridge_interrupt_assert():
 384:  */
 385: void footbridge_interrupt_assert(struct interrupt *interrupt)
 386: {
 387: 	struct footbridge_data *d = (struct footbridge_data *) interrupt->extra;
 388: 	d->irq_status |= interrupt->line;
 389: 
 390: 	// Patch from NetBSD:
 391: 	// "fix hang interrupt issue. Always assert the irq's even if we have
 392: 	//  already asserted the global irq for the device. prevents hangs with
 393: 	//  cats."
 394: 	if ((d->irq_status & d->irq_enable) /* && !d->irq_asserted */) {
 395: 		d->irq_asserted = 1;
 396: 		INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->irq);
 397: 	}
 398: }
 399: 
 400: 
 401: /*
 402:  *  footbridge_interrupt_deassert():
 403:  */
 404: void footbridge_interrupt_deassert(struct interrupt *interrupt)
 405: {
 406: 	struct footbridge_data *d = (struct footbridge_data *) interrupt->extra;
 407: 	d->irq_status &= ~interrupt->line;
 408: 
 409: 	if (!(d->irq_status & d->irq_enable) && d->irq_asserted) {
 410: 		d->irq_asserted = 0;

   1: /*
   2:  *  Offline differential for the SHIPPED footbridge timer -- src/devices/dev_footbridge.c.
   3:  *
   4:  *  WHY THIS EXISTS.  #432 fixed a guest-reachable HOST CRASH: `random() % timer_load[i]` with
   5:  *  a guest-writable zero load is an integer division by zero, reproduced independently by two
   6:  *  seats as SIGFPE (exit 136).  A crash fix with no detector is this project's fifth vacuity
   7:  *  class, and here the detector had to be offline for a reason stronger than convenience:
   8:  *  THERE IS NO NETWINDER OR CATS RIG ANYWHERE IN regress/ -- nothing in this tree boots a
   9:  *  machine that has a footbridge.  So this is not the cheapest detector, it is the ONLY
  10:  *  possible one.
  11:  *
  12:  *  IT IS THE REPOSITORY FILE.  This driver #includes the shipped dev_footbridge.c, exactly as
  13:  *  diff_sh4_tmu.c #includes dev_sh4.c -- an identical header set, and the precedent that made
  14:  *  the feasibility question answerable at all.  Nothing is transcribed.
  15:  *
  16:  *  SEVEN STUBS, and `-Wl,--gc-sections` is LOAD-BEARING rather than cosmetic: without it the
  17:  *  link needs eleven more symbols (bus_pci_*, interrupt_*, console_start_slave,
  18:  *  machine_add_tickfunction, memory_device_register).  The trade-off, stated so it is not
  19:  *  mistaken for coverage: gc-sections drops DEVINIT, so this file cannot test devinit.
  20:  *
  21:  *  THE TWO STUBS THAT ARE INSTRUMENTS, not scaffolding: timer_add() and
  22:  *  timer_update_frequency() RECORD the frequency the device asked for.  That is what lets a
  23:  *  row assert the rate semantics directly, instead of inferring them from a boot that cannot
  24:  *  happen here.
  25:  *

  27:  *  symbol the code itself uses.  R4's review established that a row which takes its expected
  28:  *  value from the code under test is green for ANY value of it (filed as `constblind`), so

  72: uint64_t memory_readmax64(struct cpu *cpu, unsigned char *buf, int len)
  73: {
  74: 	uint64_t x = 0; int i;
  75: 	(void) cpu;
  76: 	/*  Little-endian, which is what the rigs are; the device only ever reads back what
  77: 	    this driver wrote, so the convention only has to be self-consistent.  */
  78: 	for (i = 0; i < len; i++)

 111: /*  #545 repair 3, X14: every other block's instance is a calloc with its interrupt fields seeded, counting calls

 113:     instances here instead: the CPU line on its OWN pair, optionally DEVINIT's timer reset values, and a device

 146: static int rows, fails;
 147: static void check(const char *name, const char *got, const char *want)
 148: {
 149: 	rows++;
 150: 	if (strcmp(got, want) == 0)
 151: 		printf("  ok    %-54s %s\n", name, got);
 152: 	else {
 153: 		fails++;
 154: 		printf("  FAIL  %-54s\n          got  %s\n          want %s\n",
 155: 		    name, got, want);
 156: 	}
 157: }
 158: static void check_u(const char *name, uint64_t got, uint64_t want)
 159: {
 160: 	char g[32], w[32];
 161: 	snprintf(g, sizeof(g), "%" PRIu64, got);
 162: 	snprintf(w, sizeof(w), "%" PRIu64, want);
 163: 	check(name, g, w);
 164: }

 166: /*  The helper is static inside the shipped file, which is exactly why this driver includes
 167:     the file rather than linking it.  */
 168: int main(void)
 169: {
 170: 	struct footbridge_data *d;
 171: 	struct cpu *cpu;
 172: 	struct machine *machine;
 173: 
 174: 	/*  LINE-BUFFERED ON PURPOSE.  gate_offline.sh redirects this binary's stdout to a file,
 175: 	    which makes it FULLY buffered -- so when a mutant re-introduces the crash, the log is
 176: 	    0 BYTES and the operator gets a red row with nothing in it.  Measured: line-buffered,
 177: 	    the same run leaves 943 bytes naming A1/B2/D1 as FAIL before it dies.  The gate's
 178: 	    greps fail either way, so this is legibility rather than detection -- but it is this
 179: 	    project's own recorded stdout-buffering trap, in a new place.  */
 180: 	setvbuf(stdout, NULL, _IOLBF, 0);
 181: 
 182: 	cpu = calloc(1, sizeof(struct cpu));
 183: 	machine = calloc(1, sizeof(struct machine));
 184: 	d = calloc(1, sizeof(struct footbridge_data));
 185: 	cpu->machine = machine;
 186: 	machine->emulated_hz = 100000000;	/*  100 MHz, an ordinary rate  */
 187: 
 188: 	{
 189: 		/*
 190: 		 *  SELFCHECK -- CAN THIS FILE'S COMPARATOR STILL FAIL?
 191: 		 *
 192: 		 *  Feed check() deliberate mismatches and require the failure counter to move,
 193: 		 *  then un-record them so the rows are free.  If check() has been stubbed,
 194: 		 *  neutered, or had its comparison edited away, EVERY other row in this file is
 195: 		 *  silently inert and the gate cannot tell -- measured: 118 rows across five
 196: 		 *  detectors stayed green under exactly that stub, row counts, identity rows and
 197: 		 *  verdict tokens all intact.
 198: 		 *
 199: 		 *  TWO MISMATCHES, ONE ALPHABETIC AND ONE DIGIT-LEADING, and the second is not
 200: 		 *  padding.  The first version fed only "a" vs "b", and a pass-2 seat defeated it
 201: 		 *  with one line -- `isdigit(got[0]) || strcmp(...) == 0` -- because EVERY REAL ROW
 202: 		 *  IN THIS FILE COMPARES NUMBERS.  118 rows went inert, this sentinel stayed blind,
 203: 		 *  and four of the five gate-2 self-mutants stayed green too.  A sentinel whose
 204: 		 *  input does not resemble the rows it vouches for is a sentinel with a whitelist.
 205: 		 *
 206: 		 *  THE HEALTHY PATH PRINTS AN ok-SHAPED LINE, DELIBERATELY.  The first version
 207: 		 *  printed nothing on success and a FAIL line only on failure, which broke two
 208: 		 *  things at once: there was no stable string for gate 2 to grep, so DELETING THIS
 209: 		 *  WHOLE BLOCK FROM ALL FIVE FILES left the gate at PASS with the same 220 checks
 210: 		 *  and zero red rows -- and the FAIL text it printed on the HEALTHY path polluted
 211: 		 *  the gate's kill attribution, so a self-mutant pinned to a row id like "fail"
 212: 		 *  matched this line and reported OK for a mutant nothing had caught.
 213: 		 *
 214: 		 *  `>` not `== +1`: a comparator that double-counts compares perfectly well, and
 215: 		 *  the exact form printed "no longer compares" at it.  Direction was safe, the
 216: 		 *  diagnosis was wrong.
 217: 		 *
 218: 		 *  This does NOT prove any row is correct.  It proves the comparator still fails on
 219: 		 *  inputs shaped like the ones the rows use.  A comparator that compares
 220: 		 *  SELECTIVELY -- or that special-cases this row by name -- is still invisible to
 221: 		 *  it, and only a census would see that.  Liveness sentinel, nothing more.
 222: 		 *
 223: 		 *  THE PROBE ROWS ARE NAMED @@SELFCHECK@@ SO THEY CANNOT BE MISTAKEN FOR A ROW.
 224: 		 *  They are deliberate mismatches, so check() prints FAIL for them on the HEALTHY
 225: 		 *  path -- and a pass-2 seat measured that a self-mutant pinned to a row id like
 226: 		 *  "fail" matched one of those lines and reported OK for a mutant nothing caught.
 227: 		 *  The obvious cure is worse than the disease: suppressing the print INSIDE check()
 228: 		 *  would need a name test in the comparator, which is precisely the whitelist the
 229: 		 *  same seat showed defeats this sentinel (`strncmp(name,"SELFCHECK",9)`).  So the
 230: 		 *  comparator is left alone and the probe rows carry a token no real row would use;
 231: 		 *  selfmutant.py filters it, and gate 2 greps the SUMMARY line below for presence.
 232: 		 */
 233: 		int selfcheck_f = fails, selfcheck_r = rows, selfcheck_bad = 0;
 234: 
 235: 		check("@@SELFCHECK@@/str", "a", "b");
 236: 		if (fails <= selfcheck_f)
 237: 			selfcheck_bad++;
 238: 		fails = selfcheck_f; rows = selfcheck_r;
 239: 
 240: 		check("@@SELFCHECK@@/num", "1", "2");
 241: 		if (fails <= selfcheck_f)
 242: 			selfcheck_bad++;
 243: 		fails = selfcheck_f; rows = selfcheck_r;
 244: 
 245: 		if (selfcheck_bad) {
 246: 			printf("  FAIL  SELFCHECK the comparator no longer compares (%d of 2 "
 247: 			    "mismatches went unnoticed) -- every row in this file is inert\n",
 248: 			    selfcheck_bad);
 249: 			fails = selfcheck_f + 1;
 250: 			rows = selfcheck_r;
 251: 		} else {
 252: 			printf("  ok    SELFCHECK the comparator can still fail       "
 253: 			    "str+num\n");
 254: 		}
 255: 	}
 256: 
 257: 	printf("--- A. the load interpretation: zero is a FULL PERIOD, never a divisor of 0 ---\n");
 258: 	/*  16777216 == 2^24, written as an absolute rather than read from TIMER_MAX_VAL: a row
 259: 	    that takes its expected value from the code under test cannot notice the code
 260: 	    changing (the `constblind` finding).  */
 261: 	check_u("A1 load 0 becomes the full 24-bit span",
 262: 	    footbridge_effective_cycles(0, 0, 0), 16777216);
 263: 	check_u("A2 an ordinary load is untouched",
 264: 	    footbridge_effective_cycles(1000, 0, 0), 1000);
 265: 	check_u("A3 the maximum load is untouched",
 266: 	    footbridge_effective_cycles(0xffffff, 0, 0), 16777215);
 267: 
 268: 	printf("--- B. the prescaler is 64-bit MULTIPLICATION, not a signed shift ---\n");
 269: 	/*  THE ROW THAT CATCHES THE DEFECT THE OBVIOUS FIX RE-CREATES.  The old code used
 270: 	    `int cycles` with `cycles <<= 8`; three seats found independently that this
 271: 	    overflows for exactly half the legal range, and that mapping zero to 2^24 while
 272: 	    leaving `int` gives 2^24 << 8 == 2^32 == 0 -> +INF again.  B1 and B2 are the two
 273: 	    halves of that: the reset load prescaled, and the zero load prescaled.  */
 274: 	check_u("B1 reset load x256 does not overflow",
 275: 	    footbridge_effective_cycles(0xffffff, TIMER_FCLK_256, 1),
 276: 	    (uint64_t) 16777215 * 256);
 277: 	check_u("B2 zero load x256 does not wrap to zero",

 280: 	check_u("B3 the x16 prescaler", footbridge_effective_cycles(1000, TIMER_FCLK_16, 1),
 281: 	    16000);

 284: 	/*  Both prescaler bits set is TIMER_EXTERNAL (0x0C).  The order of the tests matters:
 285: 	    with a bare `else if` chain, 0x0C reads as FCLK_16 by accident.  */
 286: 	check_u("C1 TIMER_EXTERNAL is not silently read as x16",
 287: 	    footbridge_effective_cycles(1000, TIMER_FCLK_16 | TIMER_FCLK_256, 1), 1000);
 288: 
 289: 	printf("--- D. THE TWO CONSUMERS LIVE IN DIFFERENT DOMAINS ---\n");
 290: 	/*  THE ROW A TENTH READING FORCED.  timer_value is a 24-bit GUEST-READABLE register.
 291: 	    Feeding it the PRESCALED span would store values far above 24 bits -- the measuring
 292: 	    seat's own first proposal did exactly that and put 19833 of 20000 ticks out of
 293: 	    range.  The prescaler slows the RATE; it does not widen the COUNTER.  */
 294: 	check_u("D1 the counter domain ignores the prescaler",
 295: 	    footbridge_effective_cycles(0, TIMER_FCLK_256, 0), 16777216);
 296: 	{

 299: 		d->timer_load[0] = 0;
 300: 		d->timer_control[0] = TIMER_ENABLE | TIMER_FCLK_256;
 301: 		d->pending_timer_interrupts[0] = 1;
 302: 		for (i = 0; i < 20000; i++) {

 330: 	memset(d, 0, sizeof(*d));

 337: 	/*  E2 is the route that needs NO load write at all: the reset load is TIMER_MAX_VAL,
 338: 	    so one write of ENABLE|FCLK_256 reaches the old overflow.  Before #432 this asked

 340: 	    years, silently.  */

 342: 	d->timer_control[0] = TIMER_ENABLE | TIMER_FCLK_256;
 343: 	last_freq = -1.0;
 354: 	    (last_freq > 1.0 && last_freq < 100.0) ? "serviceable" : "OUT OF RANGE", 355: 	    "serviceable");
 356: 	/*  E4 EXISTS BECAUSE E2 WAS A SIGN-ONLY ORACLE, and the review measured the cost of
 357: 	    that: changing the `1` in reload_timer_value()'s helper call to a `0` -- ONE

 401: 	    proves the guest can actually WRITE it.  Before #432 this exact write reached
 402: 	    fatal()+exit(1) in the CONTROL arm -- a guest selecting the external clock source
 403: 	    killed the host, and no amount of helper correctness would have saved it.  Forked,
 404: 	    because a mutant that restores the exit(1) would otherwise take the gate binary

 407: 		pid_t pid = fork();

 417: 			_exit(0);

 426: 
 427: 	printf("--- H. the LOAD register reads back EXACTLY what the guest wrote ---\n");
 428: 	/*  THE FIFTH-VACUITY-CLASS ROW, and the adjudicating seat found it by attacking its own
 429: 	    design: "timer_load[] is never rewritten -- the guest reads back exactly what it
 430: 	    wrote" is a shipped, load-bearing claim, and it had NO DETECTOR.  Reinstating
 431: 	    normalize-at-write -- `timer_load = idata ? idata : 1`, precisely the alternative
 432: 	    this round REJECTED -- passed all fifteen rows, because nothing in this file ever
 433: 	    drove TIMER_1_LOAD through the access handler; every other row pokes struct fields
 434: 	    directly.  These two rows go through dev_footbridge_access in both directions.  */
 435: 	{
 436: 		struct footbridge_data *h = calloc(1, sizeof(*h));
 437: 		unsigned char buf[4];
 438: 		uint64_t back;
 439: 		arm_irqs(h);
 440: 
 441: 		memset(buf, 0, sizeof(buf));
 442: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_LOAD, buf, 4, MEM_WRITE, h);
 443: 		memset(buf, 0xff, sizeof(buf));
 444: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_LOAD, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
 445: 		back = memory_readmax64(cpu, buf, 4);
 446: 		check_u("H1 a guest write of 0 reads back as 0, not normalised", back, 0);
 447: 
 448: 		/*  H2 pins the 24-bit MASK as well: 0x1000000 is "one full wrap", and the mask
 449: 		    aliases it to zero -- which is the arithmetic that makes the full-period
 450: 		    mapping the continuous choice in the first place.  */

 454: 		memset(buf, 0xff, sizeof(buf));
 455: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_LOAD, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
 456: 		back = memory_readmax64(cpu, buf, 4);
 457: 		check_u("H2 0x1000000 is masked to 0, and stays 0 on read-back", back, 0);
 458: 		free(h);
