 460: 
 461: 	/*  fbclearread: `case TIMER_1_CLEAR:` carries NO writeflag test, while the case
 462: 	    immediately above it (TIMER_1_CONTROL) does.  So a guest READ runs the whole
 463: 	    body: it reloads the counter when periodic, decrements the pending count, and
 464: 	    DEASSERTS the interrupt.  A guest that merely reads the register silently
 465: 	    acknowledges a pending timer interrupt -- the likely guest-visible consequence
 466: 	    is a LOST interrupt, not a corrupted value.
 467: 
 468: 	    FOUR addresses, not one: the timer fold normalises 0x30C/0x32C/0x34C/0x36C onto
 469: 	    this single case.
 470: 
 471: 	    These rows drive the ACCESS HANDLER rather than poking struct fields, for the
 472: 	    reason this file already records above: a struct-poking row cannot see a
 473: 	    handler-level defect at all.  */
 474: 	{
 475: 		struct footbridge_data *h = calloc(1, sizeof(*h));
 476: 		unsigned char buf[4];
 477: 		uint64_t back;
 478: 
 479: 		arm_irqs(h);
 480: 
 481: 		/*  CONTROL first: a WRITE must decrement.  Without it a green defect row
 482: 		    could equally mean the handler never ran.  */
 483: 		h->pending_timer_interrupts[0] = 2;
 484: 		memset(buf, 0, sizeof(buf));
 485: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_CLEAR, buf, 4, MEM_WRITE, h);
 486: 		check_u("[LIVE] a WRITE of TIMER_1_CLEAR decrements pending",
 487: 		    h->pending_timer_interrupts[0], 1);
 488: 
 489: 		/*  THE DEFECT: a READ must leave the pending count alone.  */
 490: 		h->pending_timer_interrupts[0] = 2;
 491: 		memset(buf, 0xff, sizeof(buf));
 492: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_CLEAR, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
 493: 		/*  THE GUARD WRAPS THREE EFFECTS AND THE ROWS ABOVE OBSERVE TWO.
 494: 		    reload_timer_value and the pending decrement are both pinned;
 495: 		    INTERRUPT_DEASSERT was not.  A panel seat named the mutant that
 496: 		    exploits the gap -- hoist the deassert OUT of the guard so a READ
 497: 		    still deasserts:
 498: 
 499: 		        if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) { reload; pending--; }
 500: 		        INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->timer_irq[timer_nr]);
 501: 
 502: 		    Every other row passes -- the deassert touches neither the pending
 503: 		    count nor timer_value -- while the guest READ once again
 504: 		    acknowledges a pending interrupt.  **That is #474's headline
 505: 		    symptom, reintroduced**: the round's own record calls the
 506: 		    consequence a LOST interrupt, and a lost interrupt IS a spurious
 507: 		    deassert.
 508: 
 509: 		    The counter was already armed by noop_deassert and NEVER READ.
 510: 		    A counter nothing asserts on is a row that does not exist.  */
 511: 		/*  THE ACCEPT COMPANION, and the row below is HALF A ROW without it.
 512: 		    A row that wants ZERO can only fail UPWARD: delete
 513: 		    INTERRUPT_DEASSERT from the guarded body outright and the READ
 514: 		    row stays green, because zero deasserts is exactly what it
 515: 		    asserts.  Measured by a panel seat against the version that
 516: 		    shipped this row without its pair.
 517: 
 518: 		    This is the de-escalation clause's accept side in miniature:
 519: 		    the fix made a channel CONDITIONAL, so the detector owes a row
 520: 		    proving the channel still fires on the legal path.  */
 521: 		irq_deasserts = 0;
 522: 		h->pending_timer_interrupts[0] = 2;
 523: 		memset(buf, 0, sizeof(buf));
 524: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_CLEAR, buf, 4, MEM_WRITE, h);
 525: 		check_u("[LIVE] a WRITE DOES deassert the irq", irq_deasserts, 1);
 526: 
 527: 		irq_deasserts = 0;
 528: 		h->pending_timer_interrupts[0] = 2;
 529: 		memset(buf, 0xff, sizeof(buf));
 530: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_CLEAR, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
 531: 		check_u("a READ must NOT deassert the irq", irq_deasserts, 0);
 532: 
 533: 		check_u("a READ of TIMER_1_CLEAR must NOT clear pending",
 534: 		    h->pending_timer_interrupts[0], 2);
 535: 
 536: 		/*  ENABLE.  A measuring seat named this the worst of five one-token
 537: 		    escapes: widen the guard with `|| (timer_control & TIMER_ENABLE)`
 538: 		    and it sweeps the whole table, because every other row here uses
 539: 		    control = 0 or PERIODIC and never TIMER_ENABLE.
 540: 
 541: 		    It is the worst because of WHICH state it re-opens: a DISABLED
 542: 		    timer can never hold a pending interrupt, so the mutant re-opens
 543: 		    the defect on precisely the state this round exists for.  */
 544: 		h->timer_control[0] = TIMER_ENABLE;
 545: 		h->pending_timer_interrupts[0] = 2;
 546: 		memset(buf, 0xff, sizeof(buf));
 547: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_CLEAR, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
 548: 		check_u("an ENABLED timer READ does not clear",
 549: 		    h->pending_timer_interrupts[0], 2);
 550: 		h->timer_control[0] = 0;
 551: 
 552: 		/*  WIDTH.  `&& len == 4` sweeps every row above -- they all use a
 553: 		    four-byte access.  The same shape as the wdc detector's own width
 554: 		    hole, found the same day in a different file.  */
 555: 		h->pending_timer_interrupts[0] = 2;
 556: 		memset(buf, 0xff, sizeof(buf));
 557: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_CLEAR, buf, 2, MEM_READ, h);
 558: 		check_u("a 2-byte READ does not clear either",
 559: 		    h->pending_timer_interrupts[0], 2);
 560: 
 561: 		/*  *** THE ANCHOR ROWS, and the premise they correct is mine. ***
 562: 		    This round's record said 'every row exercises timer 1'.  It does
 563: 		    not: TIMER_1_CLEAR is 0x30C and (0x30C >> 5) & 3 == 0, so every
 564: 		    row above is an INDEX-0 row.  That is exactly why `timer_nr = 0`
 565: 		    and a widened fold mask each score a clean sweep -- the file
 566: 		    claims FOUR addresses and carried no row distinguishing any of
 567: 		    them.
 568: 
 569: 		    0x32C selects index 1 and 0x36C index 3.  Asserting the NEIGHBOUR
 570: 		    is untouched is what makes these ANCHORED rather than merely
 571: 		    distinct: a re-map that collides on one probed cell still reds.
 572: 		    */
 573: 		h->pending_timer_interrupts[0] = 5;
 574: 		h->pending_timer_interrupts[1] = 2;
 575: 		memset(buf, 0, sizeof(buf));
 576: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_2_CLEAR, buf, 4, MEM_WRITE, h);
 577: 		check_u("0x32C decrements timer index 1",
 578: 		    h->pending_timer_interrupts[1], 1);
 579: 		check_u("...and leaves index 0 untouched",
 580: 		    h->pending_timer_interrupts[0], 5);
 581: 
 582: 		h->pending_timer_interrupts[3] = 2;
 583: 		memset(buf, 0, sizeof(buf));
 584: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_4_CLEAR, buf, 4, MEM_WRITE, h);
 585: 		check_u("0x36C decrements timer index 3",
 586: 		    h->pending_timer_interrupts[3], 1);
 587: 		check_u("...and index 0 is still untouched",
 588: 		    h->pending_timer_interrupts[0], 5);
 589: 
 590: 		/*  A READ AT A NON-ZERO INDEX, and it is the row the anchor pair
 591: 		    above could not be: those drive WRITES, so a guard widened by
 592: 		    `|| timer_nr != 0` passes them for the ordinary reason and
 593: 		    nothing reds.  MEASURED: that mutant escaped the 32-row table
 594: 		    until this row existed.
 595: 
 596: 		    The general shape is worth naming: an ANCHOR row proves WHICH
 597: 		    cell an access lands on, and a GUARD row proves the access is
 598: 		    refused.  A mutant that widens the guard per-index needs both
 599: 		    properties in ONE row, and neither existing row had them.  */
 600: 		h->pending_timer_interrupts[1] = 2;
 601: 		memset(buf, 0xff, sizeof(buf));
 602: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_2_CLEAR, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
 603: 		check_u("a READ at index 1 does not clear",
 604: 		    h->pending_timer_interrupts[1], 2);
 605: 
 606: 		/*  THE COMMON TAIL, and it is the row the fix's own comment needed.
 607: 		    That comment says the `break` is deliberate because an early
 608: 		    `return` would skip
 609: 		        if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
 610: 		                memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len, odata);
 611: 		    and hand a guest an untouched buffer.  Nothing tested that: buf was
 612: 		    memset to 0xff and never read, so `break` -> `return 1` passed both
 613: 		    rows.  A stated rationale with no row behind it is a claim, not a
 614: 		    property.  Same access as the row above -- the question is what that
 615: 		    call LEFT IN THE BUFFER, not what it did to the device.
 616: 
 617: 		    Zero is the tail writing an untouched odata, which is also what the
 618: 		    pre-fix code returned; the datasheet specifies no read value for a
 619: 		    write-only register, so this pins DETERMINISM, not architecture.  */
 620: 		back = memory_readmax64(cpu, buf, 4);
 621: 		check_u("a READ still runs the common tail", back, 0);
 622: 
 623: 		/*  PERIODIC READ.  The rows above use control = 0, so the periodic arm
 624: 		    of the guarded body never runs and a guard widened to admit a
 625: 		    periodic read still passes them.  reload_timer_value() assigns
 626: 		    timer_value = timer_load, so a value deliberately UNEQUAL to load
 627: 		    makes the reload observable with no clock and no tick.
 628: 
 629: 		    This is the clause-(d) row for this fix: it keeps the guard and
 630: 		    nulls its repair on the one path the other rows cannot reach.  */
 631: 		h->timer_control[0] = TIMER_MODE_PERIODIC;
 632: 		h->timer_load[0]    = 0x1234;
 633: 		h->timer_value[0]   = 0x9999;	/*  deliberately != load  */
 634: 		h->pending_timer_interrupts[0] = 2;
 635: 		memset(buf, 0xff, sizeof(buf));
 636: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_CLEAR, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
 637: 		check_u("a PERIODIC read must not reload the counter",
 638: 		    h->timer_value[0], 0x9999);
 639: 		check_u("a PERIODIC read must not clear pending either",
 640: 		    h->pending_timer_interrupts[0], 2);
 641: 
 642: 		free(h);
 643: 	}
 644: 
 645: 	/*
 646: 	 *  #545 P-ROWS -- the five undefined-direction accesses that used to end the process are
 647: 	 *  DECLINED with a latched announce.  These rows pin what no stepped guest can (the guest
 648: 	 *  rows live in footbridge_sites_probe.py, arm S): PRESERVATION under a NONZERO status, the
 649: 	 *  read EXPRESSIONS, the widths, and the latch -- fatal() is a counter here, so the announce
 650: 	 *  is counted, never read (the misname check is the guest rows' job).
 651: 	 *
 652: 	 *  SEEDED BY DIRECT FIELD WRITE, NEVER THROUGH +0x188/+0x18c.  A bare calloc'd instance has
 653: 	 *  NULL d->irq callbacks and the enable arms call INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->irq), so this block
 654: 	 *  installs counting no-ops FIRST; the seeds are still written directly, so that no enable-
 655: 	 *  arm assert is mixed into the interrupt-output counts the rows below compare.
 656: 	 *  Seeds (enumerated, pass 2): 0x18/0x30/0x60/0xc0 -- every field-for-field swap in either
 657: 	 *  status expression yields a DIFFERENT value (0x50 collided: 0x50 & 0x30 == 0x18 & 0x30).
 658: 	 *  fiq_status has no writer in the product; seeding it pins the +0x280/+0x284 expressions
 659: 	 *  against a value the product never produces -- a NARROWING of that gap, not its closure.
 660: 	 */
 661: 	{
 662: 		struct footbridge_data *h = calloc(1, sizeof(struct footbridge_data));
 663: 		unsigned char buf[8];
 664: 		char nm[96], nm2[128];
 665: 		int f0, i;
 666: 		static const struct { uint64_t addr; int wf; int len; const char *what; } dec[] = {
 667: 			{ IRQ_STATUS,       MEM_WRITE, 4, "P1 write IRQ_STATUS"       },
 668: 			{ IRQ_RAW_STATUS,   MEM_WRITE, 4, "P2 write IRQ_RAW_STATUS"   },
 669: 			{ IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, MEM_READ,  4, "P3 read IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR"  },
 670: 			{ FIQ_STATUS,       MEM_WRITE, 4, "P4 write FIQ_STATUS"       },
 671: 			{ FIQ_RAW_STATUS,   MEM_WRITE, 4, "P5 write FIQ_RAW_STATUS"   },
 672: 		};
 673: 		arm_irqs(h);
 674: 		h->irq.interrupt_assert = noop_assert;
 675: 		h->irq.interrupt_deassert = noop_deassert;
 676: 		/*  seeds chosen so MASKED != RAW: a dropped `&` (raw returned as status) reds P0  */
 677: 		h->irq_status = 0x18;  h->irq_enable = 0x30;	/*  masked 0x10, raw 0x18  */
 678: 		h->fiq_status = 0x60;  h->fiq_enable = 0xc0;	/*  masked 0x40, raw 0x60  */
 679: 		fatal_calls = 0;	/*  this block counts announces from a clean baseline  */
 680: #define P_STATE_OK(tag) do { \
 681: 		snprintf(nm2, sizeof(nm2), "%s -- irq_status preserved", tag); check_u(nm2, h->irq_status, 0x18); \
 682: 		snprintf(nm2, sizeof(nm2), "%s -- irq_enable preserved", tag); check_u(nm2, h->irq_enable, 0x30); \
 683: 		snprintf(nm2, sizeof(nm2), "%s -- fiq_status preserved", tag); check_u(nm2, h->fiq_status, 0x60); \
 684: 		snprintf(nm2, sizeof(nm2), "%s -- fiq_enable preserved", tag); check_u(nm2, h->fiq_enable, 0xc0); } while (0)
 685: #define PN(suffix) (snprintf(nm, sizeof(nm), "%s: %s", dec[i].what, suffix), nm)
 686: 		/*  the legal reads FIRST, on the seeded state: the expressions, not a constant 0  */
 687: 		memset(buf, 0, sizeof(buf));
 688: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, IRQ_STATUS, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
 689: 		check_u("P0 IRQ_STATUS read = irq_status & irq_enable (0x10)", memory_readmax64(cpu, buf, 4), 0x10);
 690: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, IRQ_RAW_STATUS, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
 691: 		check_u("P0 IRQ_RAW_STATUS read = irq_status (0x18)", memory_readmax64(cpu, buf, 4), 0x18);
 692: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, FIQ_STATUS, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
 693: 		check_u("P0 FIQ_STATUS read = fiq_status & fiq_enable (0x40)", memory_readmax64(cpu, buf, 4), 0x40);
 694: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, FIQ_RAW_STATUS, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
 695: 		check_u("P0 FIQ_RAW_STATUS read = fiq_status (0x60)", memory_readmax64(cpu, buf, 4), 0x60);
 696: 		check_u("P0 the legal reads announce nothing", fatal_calls, 0);
 697: 		for (i = 0; i < 5; i++) {
 698: 			int a0 = irq_asserts, d0 = irq_deasserts, rv;
 699: 			f0 = fatal_calls;
 700: 			memory_writemax64(cpu, buf, 4, 0x70);	/*  bits in BOTH enables: 0x70&0x30=0x30, 0x70&0xc0=0x40  */
 701: 			rv = dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, dec[i].addr, buf, dec[i].len, dec[i].wf, h);
 702: 			check_u(PN("handler returned 1 (declined, not failed)"), rv, 1);
 703: 			check_u(PN("announced exactly once"), fatal_calls - f0, 1);
 704: 			if (dec[i].wf == MEM_READ)
 705: 				check_u(PN("the declined read returns 0"), memory_readmax64(cpu, buf, 4), 0);
 706: 			P_STATE_OK(PN("after #1"));
 707: 			check_u(PN("interrupt output untouched"), (irq_asserts - a0) + (irq_deasserts - d0), 0);
 708: 			f0 = fatal_calls;
 709: 			memory_writemax64(cpu, buf, 4, 0x70);
 710: 			dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, dec[i].addr, buf, dec[i].len, dec[i].wf, h);
 711: 			check_u(PN("#2 announced nothing more (latched)"), fatal_calls - f0, 0);
 712: 			memory_writemax64(cpu, buf, 4, 0x70);
 713: 			dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, dec[i].addr, buf, 1, dec[i].wf, h);
 714: 			memory_writemax64(cpu, buf, 4, 0x70);
 715: 			dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, dec[i].addr, buf, 2, dec[i].wf, h);
 716: 			check_u(PN("byte and halfword accesses: still latched"), fatal_calls - f0, 0);
 717: 			P_STATE_OK(PN("after #2, byte, halfword"));
 718: 		}
 719: 		/*  the latches are per SITE: five accesses, five announces, none shared  */
 720: 		check_u("P6 five sites announced five times in one instance", fatal_calls, 5);
 721: 		/*  a legal read after the declines still reads the seeded expressions  */
 722: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, IRQ_STATUS, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
 723: 		check_u("P7 IRQ_STATUS read after the declines (0x10)", memory_readmax64(cpu, buf, 4), 0x10);
 724: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, FIQ_RAW_STATUS, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
 725: 		check_u("P7 FIQ_RAW_STATUS read after the declines (0x60)", memory_readmax64(cpu, buf, 4), 0x60);
 726: 		/*  a LEGAL EnableClear write still works, next to the declined read of the same address  */
 727: 		memory_writemax64(cpu, buf, 4, 0x10);
 728: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, buf, 4, MEM_WRITE, h);
 729: 		check_u("P8 legal EnableClear write: 0x30 & ~0x10", h->irq_enable, 0x20);
 730: 		check_u("P8 ... announced nothing", fatal_calls, 5);
 731: #undef PN
 732: #undef P_STATE_OK
 733: 		free(h);
 734: 		/*  P9: a FRESH instance whose FIRST access to a site is a BYTE write.  A decline that fires only
 735: 		    for len == 4 would let the byte fall through silently -- indistinguishable above, where the
 736: 		    word access had already latched the site.  Here it must announce.  */
 737: 		h = calloc(1, sizeof(struct footbridge_data));
 738: 		arm_irqs(h);
 739: 		h->irq.interrupt_assert = noop_assert;
 740: 		h->irq.interrupt_deassert = noop_deassert;
 741: 		h->irq_status = 0x18;  h->irq_enable = 0x30;
 742: 		fatal_calls = 0;
 743: 		memory_writemax64(cpu, buf, 1, 0x70);
 744: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, IRQ_STATUS, buf, 1, MEM_WRITE, h);
 745: 		check_u("P9 a BYTE write as the first access is declined AND announced", fatal_calls, 1);
 746: 		check_u("P9 ... and stores nothing", h->irq_status, 0x18);
 747: 		free(h);
 748: 		fatal_calls = 0;
 749: 	}
 750: 
 751: 	/*  #545 pass 2 (L2 floor repair, measured by the Opus seat): the P-rows above pinned width and latch at ONE site
 752: 	    each, so a `len == 4` conjunct on any of the other four decline guards, a legal read that re-arms a latch, and
 753: 	    a decline guard widened to swallow a LEGAL narrow read all passed every row.  X1: every site at len 1 and 2 as
 754: 	    the FIRST access announces once (and the narrow declined read returns 0).  X2: legal narrow reads of the
 755: 	    five readable registers return the low lanes and announce nothing.  X3: legal reads of every sibling at every
 756: 	    width, legal enable writes, and reads of other registers re-arm NO latch.  X4/X5 (pass 2, codex): ZERO data
 757: 	    or a zero enable as the first access still declines AND announces; a legal zero Clear write does not.  */
 758: 	{
 759: 		static const struct { uint64_t addr; int wf; const char *what; } s5[] = {
 760: 			{ IRQ_STATUS, MEM_WRITE, "X1 write IRQ_STATUS" }, { IRQ_RAW_STATUS, MEM_WRITE, "X1 write IRQ_RAW_STATUS" },
 761: 			{ IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, MEM_READ, "X1 read IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR" }, { FIQ_STATUS, MEM_WRITE, "X1 write FIQ_STATUS" },
 762: 			{ FIQ_RAW_STATUS, MEM_WRITE, "X1 write FIQ_RAW_STATUS" } };
 763: 		static const struct { uint64_t addr; uint64_t want; const char *what; } rd5[] = {
 764: 			{ IRQ_STATUS, 0x10, "IRQ_STATUS" }, { IRQ_RAW_STATUS, 0x18, "IRQ_RAW_STATUS" },
 765: 			{ FIQ_STATUS, 0x40, "FIQ_STATUS" }, { FIQ_RAW_STATUS, 0x60, "FIQ_RAW_STATUS" },
 766: 			{ IRQ_ENABLE_SET, 0x30, "IRQ_ENABLE_SET" } };
 767: 		unsigned char b[8];
 768: 		char xn[160];
 769: 		int i, w, f0;
 770: 		struct footbridge_data *x;
 771: #define XSEED(x) do { arm_irqs(x); (x)->irq.interrupt_assert = noop_assert; (x)->irq.interrupt_deassert = noop_deassert; \
 772: 		(x)->irq_status = 0x18; (x)->irq_enable = 0x30; (x)->fiq_status = 0x60; (x)->fiq_enable = 0xc0; } while (0)
 773: 		for (w = 1; w <= 2; w++)
 774: 			for (i = 0; i < 5; i++) {
 775: 				x = calloc(1, sizeof(*x)); XSEED(x);
 776: 				fatal_calls = 0;
 777: 				memory_writemax64(cpu, b, w, 0x70);
 778: 				dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, s5[i].addr, b, w, s5[i].wf, x);
 779: 				snprintf(xn, sizeof(xn), "%s: len %d as the FIRST access announces once", s5[i].what, w);
 780: 				check_u(xn, fatal_calls, 1);
 781: 				if (s5[i].wf == MEM_READ) {	/*  the narrow declined read's VALUE (pass 2)  */
 782: 					snprintf(xn, sizeof(xn), "X1 read IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR: len %d declined read returns 0", w);
 783: 					check_u(xn, memory_readmax64(cpu, b, w), 0);
 784: 				}
 785: 				free(x);
 786: 			}

 791: 				memset(b, 0, sizeof(b));
 792: 				dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, rd5[i].addr, b, w, MEM_READ, x);
 793: 				snprintf(xn, sizeof(xn), "X2 legal len %d read of %s = low lanes", w, rd5[i].what);
 794: 				check_u(xn, memory_readmax64(cpu, b, w), rd5[i].want & (w == 1 ? 0xff : 0xffff));
 795: 			}
 796: 		check_u("X2 the legal narrow reads announce nothing", fatal_calls, 0);
 797: 		for (i = 0; i < 5; i++) {
 798: 			memory_writemax64(cpu, b, 4, 0x70);
 799: 			dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, s5[i].addr, b, 4, s5[i].wf, x);
 800: 		}
 801: 		check_u("X3 the five declines latch (5 announces)", fatal_calls, 5);
 802: 		f0 = fatal_calls;
 803: 		for (w = 1; w <= 4; w *= 2)
 804: 			for (i = 0; i < 5; i++)
 805: 				dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, rd5[i].addr, b, w, MEM_READ, x);
 806: 		for (i = 0; i < 5; i++) {
 807: 			memory_writemax64(cpu, b, 4, 0x70);
 808: 			dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, s5[i].addr, b, 4, s5[i].wf, x);
 809: 		}
 810: 		check_u("X3 legal reads of every sibling re-arm NO latch", fatal_calls - f0, 0);
 811: 		/*  X3, widened (pass 2): legal WRITES to the four enable registers, and reads of registers outside the
 812: 		    five above (VENDOR_ID, TIMER_1_VALUE, the silent FIQEnableClear read), re-arm no latch either.  */
 813: 		f0 = fatal_calls;
 814: 		memory_writemax64(cpu, b, 4, 0);
 815: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, IRQ_ENABLE_SET, b, 4, MEM_WRITE, x);
 816: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, b, 4, MEM_WRITE, x);
 817: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, FIQ_ENABLE_SET, b, 4, MEM_WRITE, x);
 818: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, FIQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, b, 4, MEM_WRITE, x);
 819: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, VENDOR_ID, b, 4, MEM_READ, x);
 820: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_VALUE, b, 4, MEM_READ, x);
 821: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, FIQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, b, 4, MEM_READ, x);
 822: 		for (i = 0; i < 5; i++) {
 823: 			memory_writemax64(cpu, b, 4, 0x70);
 824: 			dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, s5[i].addr, b, 4, s5[i].wf, x);
 825: 		}
 826: 		check_u("X3 legal ZERO-data enable writes and other-register reads re-arm NO latch", fatal_calls - f0, 0);
 827: 		free(x);
 828: 		/*  X4 (codex, pass 2): the FIRST access carries ZERO -- a zero-data write, or the +0x18c read with
 829: 		    IRQEnable 0.  A latch guard conditioned on the data (`&& idata`), a +0x18c guard conditioned on
 830: 		    irq_enable, or a direction test rewritten as `!idata`, lets such an access through without an
 831: 		    announce; each must announce once and change no field.  (A WRITE-site guard conditioned on an
 832: 		    enable -- X4 keeps the seeded enables there -- is X6's all-zero-state cells, not X4's.)  */
 833: 		for (i = 0; i < 5; i++) {
 834: 			x = calloc(1, sizeof(*x)); XSEED(x);
 835: 			if (s5[i].wf == MEM_READ)
 836: 				x->irq_enable = 0;
 837: 			fatal_calls = 0;
 838: 			memory_writemax64(cpu, b, 4, 0);
 839: 			dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, s5[i].addr, b, 4, s5[i].wf, x);
 840: 			snprintf(xn, sizeof(xn), "X4 %s: ZERO data/enable as the FIRST access announces once", s5[i].what + 3);
 841: 			check_u(xn, fatal_calls, 1);
 842: 			snprintf(xn, sizeof(xn), "X4 %s: ZERO data/enable changes no field", s5[i].what + 3);
 843: 			check_u(xn, (x->irq_status == 0x18) + (x->irq_enable == (s5[i].wf == MEM_READ ? 0u : 0x30u))
 844: 			    + (x->fiq_status == 0x60) + (x->fiq_enable == 0xc0), 4);
 845: 			free(x);
 846: 		}
 847: 		/*  X5 (codex, pass 2): a LEGAL ZERO EnableClear write is a write, not a declined read -- a direction test
 848: 		    rewritten as `idata` sends it to the decline and announces.  */
 849: 		x = calloc(1, sizeof(*x)); XSEED(x);
 850: 		fatal_calls = 0;
 851: 		memory_writemax64(cpu, b, 4, 0);
 852: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, b, 4, MEM_WRITE, x);
 853: 		check_u("X5 a legal ZERO EnableClear write announces nothing", fatal_calls, 0);
 854: 		check_u("X5 ... and leaves IRQEnable at 0x30", x->irq_enable, 0x30);
 855: #undef XSEED
 856: 		free(x);
 857: 		fatal_calls = 0;
 858: 	}
 859: 
 860: 	/*
 861: 	 *  #545 CLASS CLOSURE (repair pass 2 -- two consecutive passes found breaches of ONE class, so the
 862: 	 *  whole space is enumerated from the handler's own inputs and closed in one block; carrier rule).  Its own

 succeeded in 2625ms:
 411: 		INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->irq);
 412: 	}
 413: }
 414: 
 415: 
 416: /*
 417:  *  Reading the byte at 0x79000000 is a quicker way to figure out which ISA
 418:  *  interrupt has occurred (and acknowledging it at the same time), than
 419:  *  dealing with the legacy 0x20/0xa0 ISA ports.
 420:  */
 421: DEVICE_ACCESS(footbridge_isa)
 422: {
 423: 	/*  struct footbridge_data *d = extra;  */
 424: 	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
 425: 	int x;
 426: 
 427: 	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
 428: 		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);
 429: 		fatal("[ footbridge_isa: WARNING/TODO: write! idata = 0x%016" PRIx64" ]\n", idata);
 430: 	}
 431: 
 432: 	x = cpu->machine->isa_pic_data.last_int;
 433: 	if (x < 8)
 434: 		odata = cpu->machine->isa_pic_data.pic1->irq_base + x;
 435: 	else
 436: 		odata = cpu->machine->isa_pic_data.pic2->irq_base + x - 8;
 437: 
 438: 	if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
 439: 		memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len, odata);
 440: 
 441: 	return 1;
 442: }
 443: 
 444: 
 445: /*
 446:  *  Reset pin at ISA port 0x338, at least in the NetWinder:
 447:  *
 448:  *  TODO: NOT WORKING YET!
 449:  */
 450: DEVICE_ACCESS(footbridge_reset)
 451: {
 452: 	uint64_t idata = 0;
 453: 
 454: 	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
 455: 		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);
 456: 		if (idata & 0x40) {
 457: 			debug("[ footbridge_reset: GP16: Halting. ]\n");
 458: 			/*  #220: (Codex/Fable) cpu->running=0 already halts cleanly;
 459: 			    the col-0 exit(1) was a debug hack that kills the host.  */
 460: 			cpu->running = 0;
 461: 		}
 462: 	}
 463: 
 464: 	return 1;
 465: }
 466: 
 467: 
 468: /*
 469:  *  The Footbridge PCI configuration space is implemented as a direct memory
 470:  *  space (i.e. not one port for addr and one port for data). This function
 471:  *  translates that into bus_pci calls.
 472:  */
 473: DEVICE_ACCESS(footbridge_pci)
 474: {
 475: 	struct footbridge_data *d = (struct footbridge_data *) extra;
 476: 	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
 477: 	int bus, dev, func, reg;
 478: 
 479: 	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
 480: 		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len|MEM_PCI_LITTLE_ENDIAN);
 481: 
 482: 	/*  Decompose the (direct) address into its components:  */
 483: 	bus_pci_decompose_1(relative_addr, &bus, &dev, &func, &reg);
 484: 	bus_pci_setaddr(cpu, d->pcibus, bus, dev, func, reg);
 485: 
 486: 	if (bus == 255) {
 487: 		fatal("[ footbridge DEBUG ERROR: bus 255 unlikely,"
 488: 		    " pc (might not be updated) = 0x%08x ]\n", (int)cpu->pc);
 489: 		/*  #220: (Codex/Fable) don't exit() the host on a guest PCI
 490: 		    access to the (unlikely) bus 255; treat it as handled.  */
 491: 		return 1;
 492: 	}
 493: 
 494: 	debug("[ footbridge pci: %s bus %i, device %i, function %i, register "
 495: 	    "%i ]\n", writeflag == MEM_READ? "read from" : "write to", bus,
 496: 	    dev, func, reg);
 497: 
 498: 	bus_pci_data_access(cpu, d->pcibus, writeflag == MEM_READ?
 499: 	    &odata : &idata, len, writeflag);
 500: 
 501: 	if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
 502: 		memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len|MEM_PCI_LITTLE_ENDIAN, odata);
 503: 
 504: 	return 1;
 505: }
 506: 
 507: 
 508: DEVICE_ACCESS(footbridge)
 509: {
 510: 	struct footbridge_data *d = (struct footbridge_data *) extra;
 511: 	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
 512: 	int timer_nr = 0;
 513: 
 514: 	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
 515: 		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);
 516: 
 517: 	if (relative_addr >= TIMER_1_LOAD && relative_addr <= TIMER_4_CLEAR) {
 518: 		timer_nr = (relative_addr >> 5) & (N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS - 1);
 519: 		relative_addr &= ~0x060;
 520: 	}
 521: 
 522: 	switch (relative_addr) {
 523: 
 524: 	case VENDOR_ID:
 525: 		odata = 0x1011;  /*  DC21285_VENDOR_ID  */
 526: 		break;
 527: 
 528: 	case DEVICE_ID:
 529: 		odata = 0x1065;  /*  DC21285_DEVICE_ID  */
 530: 		break;
 531: 
 532: 	case 0x04:
 533: 	case 0x0c:
 534: 	case 0x10:
 535: 	case 0x14:
 536: 	case 0x18:
 537: 		/*  TODO. Written to by Linux.  */
 538: 		break;
 539: 
 540: 	case REVISION:
 541: 		odata = 3;  /*  footbridge revision number  */
 542: 		break;
 543: 
 544: 	case PCI_ADDRESS_EXTENSION:
 545: 		/*  TODO: Written to by Linux.  */
 546: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE && idata != 0)
 547: 			fatal("[ footbridge: TODO: write to PCI_ADDRESS_"
 548: 			    "EXTENSION: 0x%llx ]\n", (long long)idata);
 549: 		break;
 550: 
 551: 	case SA_CONTROL:
 552: 		/*  Read by Linux:  */
 553: 		odata = PCI_CENTRAL_FUNCTION;
 554: 		break;
 555: 
 556: 	case UART_DATA:
 557: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
 558: 			console_putchar(d->console_handle, idata);
 559: 		break;
 560: 
 561: 	case UART_RX_STAT:
 562: 		/*  TODO  */
 563: 		odata = 0;
 564: 		break;
 565: 
 566: 	case UART_FLAGS:
 567: 		odata = UART_TX_EMPTY;
 568: 		break;
 569: 
 570: 	case IRQ_STATUS:
 571: 		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
 572: 			odata = d->irq_status & d->irq_enable;
 573: 		else if (!d->warned_irq_status_w) {
 574: 			/*  #545: read-only register (7.3.29); the write is
 575: 			    declined -- no register changes, only this site's
 576: 			    warn-once latch is set -- and announced once.  */
 577: 			d->warned_irq_status_w = 1;
 578: 			fatal("[ WARNING: footbridge write to irq status? "
 579: 			    "(once) ]\n");
 580: 		}
 581: 		break;
 582: 
 583: 	case IRQ_RAW_STATUS:
 584: 		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
 585: 			odata = d->irq_status;
 586: 		else if (!d->warned_irq_raw_w) {
 587: 			/*  #545: read-only (7.3.30); declined, announced once.  */
 588: 			d->warned_irq_raw_w = 1;
 589: 			fatal("[ footbridge write to irq_raw_status (once) ]\n");
 590: 		}
 591: 		break;
 592: 
 593: 	case IRQ_ENABLE_SET:
 594: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
 595: 			d->irq_enable |= idata;
 596: 			if (d->irq_status & d->irq_enable)
 597: 				INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->irq);
 598: 			else
 599: 				INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->irq);
 600: 		} else {
 601: 			/*  #539: a read of 188h is documented -- IRQEnable is
 602: 			    "read-only ... can only be changed by writing to
 603: 			    the EnableSet and EnableClear registers", reset 0
 604: 			    (DC21285 Table 7-10, 7.3.31).  The value below was
 605: 			    always right; the exit(1) that followed it is gone.  */
 606: 			odata = d->irq_enable;
 607: 		}
 608: 		break;
 609: 
 610: 	case IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR:
 611: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
 612: 			d->irq_enable &= ~idata;
 613: 			if (d->irq_status & d->irq_enable)
 614: 				INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->irq);
 615: 			else
 616: 				INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->irq);
 617: 		} else if (!d->warned_irq_clear_r) {
 618: 			/*  #545: IRQEnableClear is write-only (7.3.33) and Table
 619: 			    7-10 names no register at 18Ch for reads, so the read
 620: 			    is declined with 0 -- the IRQEnable value that used to
 621: 			    be computed here is 188h's answer, not 18Ch's.  */
 622: 			d->warned_irq_clear_r = 1;
 623: 			fatal("[ WARNING: footbridge read from "
 627: 
 628: 	case FIQ_STATUS:
 629: 		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
 630: 			odata = d->fiq_status & d->fiq_enable;
 631: 		else if (!d->warned_fiq_status_w) {
 632: 			/*  #545: read-only (7.3.29); declined, announced once.  */
 633: 			d->warned_fiq_status_w = 1;
 634: 			fatal("[ WARNING: footbridge write to fiq status? "
 635: 			    "(once) ]\n");
 636: 		}
 637: 		break;
 638: 
 639: 	case FIQ_RAW_STATUS:
 640: 		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
 641: 			odata = d->fiq_status;
 642: 		else if (!d->warned_fiq_raw_w) {
 643: 			/*  #545: read-only (7.3.30); declined, announced once.  */
 644: 			d->warned_fiq_raw_w = 1;
 645: 			fatal("[ footbridge write to fiq_raw_status (once) ]\n");
 646: 		}
 647: 		break;
 648: 
 649: 	case FIQ_ENABLE_SET:
 650: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
 651: 			d->fiq_enable |= idata;
 652: 		break;
 653: 
 654: 	case FIQ_ENABLE_CLEAR:
 655: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
 656: 			d->fiq_enable &= ~idata;
 657: 		break;
 658: 
 659: 	case TIMER_1_LOAD:
 660: 		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
 661: 			odata = d->timer_load[timer_nr];
 662: 		else {
 663: 			d->timer_load[timer_nr] = idata & TIMER_MAX_VAL;
 664: 			reload_timer_value(cpu, d, timer_nr);
 665: 			/*  debug("[ footbridge: timer %i (1-based), "
 666: 			    "value %i ]\n", timer_nr + 1,
 667: 			    (int)d->timer_value[timer_nr]);  */
 668: 			INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->timer_irq[timer_nr]);
 669: 		}
 670: 		break;
 671: 
 672: 	case TIMER_1_VALUE:
 673: 		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
 674: 			odata = d->timer_value[timer_nr];
 675: 		else
 676: 			d->timer_value[timer_nr] = idata & TIMER_MAX_VAL;
 677: 		break;
 678: 
 679: 	case TIMER_1_CONTROL:
 680: 		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
 681: 			odata = d->timer_control[timer_nr];
 682: 		else {
 683: 			d->timer_control[timer_nr] = idata;
 684: 			/*  #432: both prescaler bits set is TIMER_EXTERNAL
 685: 			    (0x0C, dc21285reg.h:364) -- a LEGAL named encoding,
 686: 			    and it used to reach fatal()+exit(1) right here, so a
 687: 			    guest selecting the external clock source KILLED THE
 688: 			    HOST.  Arguably likelier than the zero load, since it
 689: 			    is a documented mode rather than a malformed value.
 690: 			    The rate an external clock would actually give is
 691: 			    UNKNOWN and is NOT invented: footbridge_effective_cycles()
 692: 			    now gives the encoding a defined, survivable meaning
 693: 			    (the unprescaled period), and the real semantics are
 694: 			    filed for a round that has a datasheet.  */
 695: 			if (idata & TIMER_ENABLE) {
 696: 				reload_timer_value(cpu, d, timer_nr);
 697: 			} else {
 698: 				d->pending_timer_interrupts[timer_nr] = 0;
 699: 			}
 700: 			INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->timer_irq[timer_nr]);
 701: 		}
 702: 		break;
 703: 
 704: 	case TIMER_1_CLEAR:
 705: 		/*  #474: a READ must not perform the clear.  This case carried no
 706: 		    writeflag test at all, while TIMER_1_CONTROL immediately above it
 707: 		    does -- so a guest READ reloaded the counter, decremented the
 708: 		    pending count and DEASSERTED the interrupt.  A guest that merely
 709: 		    read the register silently acknowledged a pending timer interrupt;
 710: 		    the guest-visible consequence is a LOST interrupt, not a bad value.
 711: 
 712: 		    FOUR addresses, not one: the fold above normalises 0x30C/0x32C/
 713: 		    0x34C/0x36C onto this single case.
 714: 
 715: 		    GUARD THE BODY, KEEP THE break.  An early return here would skip
 716: 		    the function's common tail, which writes odata back on a read, and
 717: 		    would hand a guest an untouched buffer -- a defect this code does
 718: 		    not currently have.  */
 719: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
 720: 			if (d->timer_control[timer_nr] & TIMER_MODE_PERIODIC) {
 721: 				reload_timer_value(cpu, d, timer_nr);
 722: 			}
 723: 
 724: 			if (d->pending_timer_interrupts[timer_nr] > 0) {
 725: 				d->pending_timer_interrupts[timer_nr] --;
 726: 			}
 727: 
 728: 			INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->timer_irq[timer_nr]);
 729: 		}
 730: 		break;
 731: 
 732: 	case SDRAM_BA_MASK:
 733: 		if (writeflag == MEM_READ) {
 734: 			fatal("[ footbridge read from sdram_ba_mask ]\n");
 735: 			exit(1);
 736: 		} else {
 737: 			switch (idata) {
 738: 			case SDRAM_MASK_256KB:
 739: 			case SDRAM_MASK_512KB:
 740: 			case SDRAM_MASK_1MB:
 741: 			case SDRAM_MASK_2MB:
 742: 			case SDRAM_MASK_4MB:
 743: 			case SDRAM_MASK_8MB:
 744: 			case SDRAM_MASK_16MB:
 745: 			case SDRAM_MASK_32MB:
 746: 			case SDRAM_MASK_64MB:
 747: 			case SDRAM_MASK_128MB:
 748: 			case SDRAM_MASK_256MB:
 749: 				break;
 750: 			default:
 751: 				fatal("[ footbridge write to sdram_ba_mask "
 752: 				    "%#llx ]\n", (long long)idata);
 753: 				break;
 754: 			}
 755: 		}
 756: 		break;
 757: 	case SDRAM_BA_OFFSET:
 758: 		if (writeflag == MEM_READ) {
 759: 			fatal("[ footbridge read from sdram_ba_offset ]\n");
 760: 			exit(1);
 761: 		} else {
 762: 			if (idata != 0)
 763: 				fatal("[ footbridge write to sdram_ba_offset "
 764: 				    "%#llx ]\n", (long long)idata);
 765: 		}
 766: 		break;
 767: 
 768: 	default:if (writeflag == MEM_READ) {
 769: 			fatal("[ footbridge: read from 0x%x ]\n",
 770: 			    (int)relative_addr);
 771: 		} else {
 772: 			fatal("[ footbridge: write to 0x%x: 0x%llx ]\n",
 773: 			    (int)relative_addr, (long long)idata);
 774: 		}
 775: 	}
 776: 
 777: 	if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
 778: 		memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len, odata);
 779: 
 780: 	return 1;
 781: }
 782: 
 783: 
 784: DEVINIT(footbridge)
 785: {
 786: 	struct footbridge_data *d;
 787: 	char irq_path[300], irq_path_isa[400];
 788: 	uint64_t pci_addr = 0x7b000000;
 789: 	int i;
 790: 
 791: 	CHECK_ALLOCATION(d = (struct footbridge_data *) malloc(sizeof(struct footbridge_data)));
 792: 	memset(d, 0, sizeof(struct footbridge_data));
 793: 
 794: 	/*
 795: 	 *  #442: the memset leaves pending_bound[] at 0, and a bound of 0 would pin
 796: 	 *  the counter at 1 rather than bounding it.  That is UNREACHABLE today --
 797: 	 *  reload_timer_value() sets the bound, and it is also the only thing that
 798: 	 *  ever creates a struct timer, so no callback can fire before a bound
 799: 	 *  exists.  Initialised here anyway, because that is a two-step argument
 800: 	 *  about a signal handler and a later edit adding a timer elsewhere would
 801: 	 *  silently break it.
 802: 	 *
 803: 	 *  *** THE SEED IS 1, NOT THE CEILING, AND THE FIRST VERSION HAD THIS
 804: 	 *  BACKWARDS. ***  It called the ceiling "the safe default: it can never
 805: 	 *  throttle, only bound."  The ceiling is 68,157,440 -- at cats' 762.94 Hz
 806: 	 *  that is TWENTY-FIVE HOURS of un-acked backlog, i.e. pre-fix behaviour
 807: 	 *  for any run shorter than a day.  Measured: a mutant seeding exactly
 808: 	 *  that value reads 3051 on the drain probe, indistinguishable from
 809: 	 *  pre-fix.  If the scenario this seed exists for ever happened, it would
 810: 	 *  hand that timer the LARGEST bound the fix permits.  1 can only
 811: 	 *  under-deliver, which is the right direction for a value that should
 812: 	 *  never be used.
 813: 	 */
 814: 	for (i = 0; i < N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS; i++)
 815: 		d->pending_bound[i] = 1;
 816: 
 817: 	/*  Connect to the CPU which this footbridge will interrupt:  */
 818: 	INTERRUPT_CONNECT(devinit->interrupt_path, d->irq);
 819: 
 820: 	/*  DC21285 register access:  */
 821: 	memory_device_register(devinit->machine->memory, devinit->name,
 822: 	    devinit->addr, DEV_FOOTBRIDGE_LENGTH,
 823: 	    dev_footbridge_access, d, DM_DEFAULT, NULL);
 824: 
 825: 	/*  ISA interrupt status/acknowledgement:  */
 826: 	memory_device_register(devinit->machine->memory, "footbridge_isa",
 827: 	    0x79000000, 8, dev_footbridge_isa_access, d, DM_DEFAULT, NULL);
 828: 
 829: 	/*  The "fcom" console:  */
 830: 	d->console_handle = console_start_slave(devinit->machine, "fcom", 0);
 831: 
 832: 	/*  Register 32 footbridge interrupts:  */
 833: 	snprintf(irq_path, sizeof(irq_path), "%s.footbridge",
 834: 	    devinit->interrupt_path);
 835: 	for (i=0; i<32; i++) {
 836: 		struct interrupt interrupt_template;
 837: 		char tmpstr[600];
 838: 
 839: 		memset(&interrupt_template, 0, sizeof(interrupt_template));
 840: 		interrupt_template.line = (uint32_t)1 << i;
 841: 		snprintf(tmpstr, sizeof(tmpstr), "%s.%i", irq_path, i);
 842: 		interrupt_template.name = tmpstr;
 843: 
 844: 		interrupt_template.extra = d;
 845: 		interrupt_template.interrupt_assert =
 846: 		    footbridge_interrupt_assert;
 847: 		interrupt_template.interrupt_deassert =
 848: 		    footbridge_interrupt_deassert;
 849: 		interrupt_handler_register(&interrupt_template);
 850: 
 851: 		/*  Connect locally to some interrupts:  */
 852: 		if (i>=IRQ_TIMER_1 && i<=IRQ_TIMER_4)
 853: 			INTERRUPT_CONNECT(tmpstr, d->timer_irq[i-IRQ_TIMER_1]);
 854: 	}
 855: 
 856: 	switch (devinit->machine->machine_type) {
 857: 	case MACHINE_CATS:
 858: 		snprintf(irq_path_isa, sizeof(irq_path_isa), "%s.10", irq_path);
 859: 		break;
 860: 	case MACHINE_NETWINDER:
 861: 		snprintf(irq_path_isa, sizeof(irq_path_isa), "%s.11", irq_path);
 862: 		break;
 863: 	default:fatal("footbridge unimpl machine type\n");
 864: 		exit(1);
 865: 	}
 866: 
 867: 	/*  A PCI bus:  */
 868: 	d->pcibus = bus_pci_init(
 869: 	    devinit->machine,
 870: 	    irq_path,
 871: 	    0x7c000000,		/*  PCI device io offset  */
 872: 	    0x80000000,		/*  PCI device mem offset  */
 873: 	    0x00000000,		/*  PCI port base  */
 874: 	    0x00000000,		/*  PCI mem base  */
 875: 	    irq_path,		/*  PCI irq base  */
 876: 	    0x7c000000,		/*  ISA port base  */
 877: 	    0x80000000,		/*  ISA mem base  */
 878: 	    irq_path_isa);	/*  ISA port base  */
 879: 
 880: 	/*  ... with some default devices for known machine types:  */
 881: 	switch (devinit->machine->machine_type) {
 882: 	case MACHINE_CATS:
 883: 		bus_pci_add(devinit->machine, d->pcibus,
 884: 		    devinit->machine->memory, 0xc0, 7, 0, "ali_m1543");
 885: 		bus_pci_add(devinit->machine, d->pcibus,
 886: 		    devinit->machine->memory, 0xc0, 10, 0, "dec21143");
 887: 		bus_pci_add(devinit->machine, d->pcibus,
 888: 		    devinit->machine->memory, 0xc0, 16, 0, "ali_m5229");
 889: 		break;
 890: 	case MACHINE_NETWINDER:
 891: 		bus_pci_add(devinit->machine, d->pcibus,
 892: 		    devinit->machine->memory, 0xc0, 11, 0, "symphony_83c553");
 893: 		bus_pci_add(devinit->machine, d->pcibus,
 894: 		    devinit->machine->memory, 0xc0, 11, 1, "symphony_82c105");
 895: 		memory_device_register(devinit->machine->memory,
 896: 		    "footbridge_reset", 0x7c000338, 1,
 897: 		    dev_footbridge_reset_access, d, DM_DEFAULT, NULL);
 898: 		break;
 899: 	default:fatal("footbridge: unimplemented machine type.\n");
 900: 		exit(1);
 901: 	}
 902: 
 903: 	/*  PCI configuration space:  */
 904: 	memory_device_register(devinit->machine->memory,
 905: 	    "footbridge_pci", pci_addr, 0x1000000,
 906: 	    dev_footbridge_pci_access, d, DM_DEFAULT, NULL);
 907: 
 908: 	/*  Timer ticks:  */
 909: 	for (i=0; i<N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS; i++) {
 910: 		d->timer_control[i] = TIMER_MODE_PERIODIC;
 911: 		d->timer_load[i] = TIMER_MAX_VAL;
 912: 	}
 913: 
 914: 	machine_add_tickfunction(devinit->machine,
 915: 	    dev_footbridge_tick, d, DEV_FOOTBRIDGE_TICK_SHIFT);
 916: 
 917: 	devinit->return_ptr = d->pcibus;
 918: 	return 1;
 919: }

READING.  No class the contract attributes to family A is held only by family B.  Families that hold NOTHING alone of
the 434: X7, X8, the WIDE and SEEDED states, P and X1-X5 (and the sub-families X6W, X7W, X7R, X9W, X10W).  Three probes
built for the purpose show X6W, X7W and X10W each CAN hold a shape alone (N_X2, N_X3, N_X1 are held by exactly those
families) -- so they are redundant for these variants, not vacuous.  X8 is subsumed by construction: X9R's wide len-4
cells read the same five registers from the same seeds.  Two records nuances: every Clear-write width/data kill is X12's
ALONE (X9's Clear rows are len 4 with data 0/0x70), so "(X9, X12)" is a union, not a joint hold; and the WIDE state now
holds exactly one thing alone, drive-test bit 16.
CHANNEL (Q1(b)).  X9/X10/X12's "drive the interrupt output exactly once by (irq_status & the new enable)" observes
irq_asserts/irq_deasserts, which count calls on d->irq AND on timer_irq[0..3] -- the frozen harness installs the same
noop_assert/noop_deassert on both (lines 95-109 and XSET/YNEW/X9).  So that channel CANNOT DIFFER under an edit that
drives the wrong interrupt (N_I05, N_I06: green, guest-visible).  And it differs under a drive-test edit only where
(irq_status & new enable) crosses zero through a ZERO operand, or equals exactly bit 4 (seeded, Clear 0) or bit 16 (wide,
Clear 0x70 / all-ones len 1, 2).  P8 makes the one access that would discriminate `&` from `&&` (status 0x18, enable
0x30, Clear 0x10: both nonzero, disjoint) and checks only the enable.

Q2 ANSWER: N_S01, `&` -> `&&` in `if (d->irq_status & d->irq_enable)` of the +0x18c arm.  One character; guest-visible
from reset in three steps (an enabled source, a different line pending, a Clear of nothing: the CPU line goes up with no
enabled interrupt pending -- the guest cannot clear it by reading IRQ_STATUS, which reads 0).  Rows expected to stay
green: P8 "legal EnableClear write: 0x30 & ~0x10" (it makes exactly the discriminating access and checks only the enable)
and every X9/X10/X12 Clear row (each has a zero operand).  Measured: 707 rows, 0 failures.
It is not "outside the contract": THE SPACE names (irq_status & the new enable) as one of the three tests the bodies make,
and the X9 header claims those rows pin the drive by it.

THE 9 REAL BUT DISCLOSED: N_D02, N_D06 (wide data on a declined write); N_D03, N_D11, N_B10 (narrow declined data 0x55 /
odd -- covered by the parenthetical "data stays 0 / 0x70", not by the heading "WIDE DATA"); N_T07, N_T08, N_T10 (a latch
guard on a field neither branch reads); N_H01 (the address axis).  All host-visible except N_H01.

=====================================================================================================================
4. Q1(c) BY MEASUREMENT -- F7's two premises
=====================================================================================================================
(i) "no decline reads idata": CONFIRMED.  N_F10 skips the fetch for writes to +0x180; N_F11 for +0x184/+0x280/+0x284;
    N_F12 performs it on the +0x18c READ, so that decline sees buffer bytes in idata.  All three: 707/707 AND no
    divergence of any kind (exhaustive to length 3 from reset, 301 and 304 ops, + 2M random) [wit2/, wit2_noalias/].
    Control N_F13 (fetch skipped for the legal Clear write) is red on 22 rows, first P8 -- the method sees idata use.
(i') "its `&& len == 4` / `&& len != 1` forms are now RED (X12's narrow Clear writes)": CONFIRMED; held by X12 alone,
    and so is N_F08 `&& len != 2`.
(i'') "a single-field zero/nonzero condition on it is held by X9/X12's writes from the all-zero and ones states":
    TRUE for the four interrupt fields (N_F02 and N_F05 red, X9 alone; N_F03, N_F04 red) and the latches (N_F06, N_F07
    red, P8 + X10).  FALSE for any other field: N_F01 (irq_asserted), N_F09 (timer_control[0]), N_F15
    (pending_timer_interrupts[0]) all pass the 707 rows and are all guest-visible.  No state sets those fields.
(ii) "the timer fold changes none of the five offsets": CONFIRMED by enumeration and by measurement [fold_enum.out].
    0x180/0x184/0x18c/0x280/0x284: outside [0x300,0x36c]; offset & ~0x060 = itself; (offset >> 5) & 3 = 0.  Bit 7 is
    SET in all five and CLEAR in every address of the fold range, so no mask edit can map a timer address onto them.
    Variants N_H01 (fold 0..0x36c), dom_fold_from180, dom_fold_from100, N_H05, N_H02, N_H04 and the five added case labels:
    with the alias probes removed (301 ops) NONE shows any divergence at all; with them, the widened folds alias
    0x1a0/0x1c0/0x1e0 -> 0x180, 0x1a4.. -> 0x184, 0x1ac.. -> 0x18c, 0x2a0.. -> 0x280, 0x2a4.. -> 0x284 (the address axis).

=====================================================================================================================
5. SOMETHING ELSE
=====================================================================================================================
(a) WIDTH 8 IS A LIVE INPUT.  cpu_arm_instr.c decodes the `(iword & 0x0e000090) == 0x00000090` space with no architecture
    check, so LDRD/STRD run on cats' SA-110; cpu_arm_instr_loadstore.c issues them as datalen 8 after `addr &=
    ~(datalen - 1)`; memory_rw.c passes len through untouched (it truncates only at the device's end).  So +0x180 and
    +0x280 (and +0x188/+0x288) receive len 8; +0x184/+0x18c/+0x284 never do.  THE SPACE says "the width", X6's widths are
    1, 2, 4, and NOT COVERED does not mention 8.  N_L06, N_L07, N_L10 pass.
(b) THE HARNESS'S INSTANCES ARE NOT RESET-FAITHFUL.  calloc leaves timer_control/timer_load/pending_bound at 0 where
    DEVINIT sets TIMER_MODE_PERIODIC/0xffffff/1, and irq_asserted is written only by footbridge_interrupt_assert(),
    which the harness stubs.  Every condition on those fields therefore passes offline; 13 of the 28 are this class.
    The reset-value ones (N_T04, N_T05, N_F09, N_T09) diverge from the first access after DEVINIT, so the 37-row guest
    probe may catch them -- I did not run it.
(c) NOT COVERED's "a latch guard conditioned on a field neither branch reads" names one site of a wider class: the same
    conditions in the direction test, the fetch test, the drive test and a latch clearer pass too (13 of the 28).
(d) NOT COVERED's "a condition on one EXACT field value": the main loop's still-green idata_A2_ne20 is an exact DATA
    value, and it is guest-visible: W188/4=0x20 W18c/4=0x20 R188/4 reads 0x20, not 0 (replay).
(e) N_SB04 is killed by exactly ONE row: "X9 IRQ Clear write 0 (seeded, latches clear)".
(f) SCRATCH CLOSURE EXPERIMENT -- a measurement, not a patch [mkext.py, diff_ext.c 5c328e45, ext_run.out,
    ext_results.jsonl].  28 rows added before IDENTITY (735): 735/0 on the fix, no warnings.  Re-scored all 145 variants
    green on the 707: 27 of the 28 undisclosed go red (N_R05, not targeted, stays green), 4 exact-value e_dir variants go
    red incidentally, and every equivalent stays green EXCEPT N_L08 and N_L11, which only Z6's five unaligned-len-8 rows
    catch -- inputs no guest can produce, so those rows should go.  By block: Z1 (1 row: Clear 0x10 from 0x18/0x30 deasserts once)
    N_S01 N_S02; Z2 (7: one wired line alone pending and enabled, a Clear of nothing asserts) N_S06 N_S07 N_SB05-18;
    Z3 (2: a CPU-line counter NOT shared with the timer interrupts) N_I05 N_I06; Z4 (3: DEVINIT's timer values) N_T04
    N_T05 N_F09 N_T09; Z5 (3: irq_asserted raised through the real callback) N_T11 N_T12 N_F01 N_I01 N_I02 N_I04; Z6 (11:
    len 8) N_L06 N_L07 N_L10 -- five of its eleven rows use unaligned len 8, which no guest can issue: drop them;
    Z7 (1: a timer tick pending) N_T06 N_F15.
(g) MY OWN APPARATUS, three corrections made before any number here was written.  (1) The first differential search
    was depth-first and reported the first witness found as "shortest"; the class verdicts were right and the lengths
    were not.  Re-run with iterative deepening; the superseded reports are kept in wit2_dfs_superseded/.  (2) The first
    width-8 alphabet included unaligned 8-byte accesses that no guest can issue; N_L08 and N_L11 are reclassified
    equivalent-for-guests [wit2_align8/].  (3) A gated_check.out column was broken by a sed mismatch; fixed before keeping.

#include "misc.h"

struct interrupt {
	/*  Functions used to assert and deassert the interrupt.  */
	void		(*interrupt_assert)(struct interrupt *);
	void		(*interrupt_deassert)(struct interrupt *);

	/*
	 *  The interrupt "line" number, or "pin" number, is an internal number
	 *  used by interrupt_assert() and interrupt_deassert(). It may
	 *  correspond to a physical interrupt number, or it may be ignored
	 *  completely.
	 *
	 *  This can either be a small integer corresponding to the interrupt
	 *  pin number, or an entire interrupt mask (for controllers with at
	 *  most 32 interrupts).
	 */
	uint32_t	line;

	/*
	 *  The extra pointer is a pointer to the (conceptual) interrupt
	 *  handler. For a CPU interrupt, this is a pointer to the CPU. For
	 *  e.g. a 8259 interrupt, it is a pointer to the dev_8259 which will
	 *  handle it.
	 */
	void		*extra;

	/*
	 *  Actual name of the interrupt. This is a complete "path", e.g.
	 *  "machine[0].cpu[1].irq[3].isa[14]". It is used for
	 *  connecting/disconnecting, and debug output.
	 */
	char		*name;
};


/*
 *  Macros to make code using the interrupt struct a bit more consise:
 */

#define	INTERRUPT_ASSERT(istruct)	(istruct).interrupt_assert(&(istruct))
#define	INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(istruct)	(istruct).interrupt_deassert(&(istruct))

#define	INTERRUPT_CONNECT(name,istruct)		{		\
	interrupt_handler_lookup(name, &(istruct));		\
	interrupt_connect(&(istruct), 0);			\
	}

#define	INTERRUPT_CONNECT_EXCLUSIVE(name,istruct)	{	\
	interrupt_handler_lookup(name, &(istruct));		\
	interrupt_connect(&(istruct), 1);			\
	}


/*
 *  Registration of interrupt handlers:
 *
 *  Each interrupt handler (i.e. CPUs, interrupt controllers, various bus
 *  controllers) should call interrupt_handler_register() to register itself.
 */

void interrupt_handler_register(struct interrupt *templ);
void interrupt_handler_remove(const char *name);
int interrupt_handler_lookup(const char *name, struct interrupt *templ);


/*
 *  Functions used to keep track of exclusive and non-exclusive users,
 *  respectively, of an interrupt.
 *
 *  If interrupt_connect() returns, it means it succeeded. On failure, the
 *  emulator exits. There may be up to 1 exclusive user and no non-exclusive
 *  users, or 0 exclusive users and any number of non-exclusive users.
 */

void interrupt_connect(struct interrupt *i, int exclusive);
void interrupt_disconnect(struct interrupt *i, int exclusive);


#endif	/*  INTERRUPT_H  */

VERDICT: DO NOT SHIP -- the instance closure still misses observable changes inside claimed cells, and the producer pair is barely exercised.

**1. Q1 — The enumeration is incomplete.**

The five edited sites’ width domains are correct: `{1,2,4,8}` at `0x180/0x280`, `{1,2,4}` at `0x184/0x18c/0x284`. I independently checked the ARM alignment and memory-dispatch code.

The missing or insufficiently specified dimensions are:

- **Producer operations and history:** assert versus deassert; first versus repeated assertion; preserving another pending source; and the producer’s own masked-status/`irq_asserted` branch partitions. Naming the producer’s possible line numbers does not enumerate its transitions. The [producer pair](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/_scratchpad/fbd5/q2e/frozen/dev_footbridge.c:385) contains two writers of both `irq_status` and `irq_asserted`.
- **Callback destination:** CPU versus each timer interrupt, crossed with the already claimed access widths. X14 distinguishes destinations only at width 4.
- **Other instance members:** `pcibus`, `console_handle`, `timer[]`, and the callbacks, `line`, `extra`, and `name` within `irq`/`timer_irq[]`. The broad exclusion for fields neither branch reads can cover conditions on these, but THE SPACE does not actually enumerate them. They do not all require new rows under the bounded floor.
- **Output handling:** read serialization and the return value belong in the enumeration of observable behavior, even though the read-tail predicate does not dominate the declines.
- **The extra readable register:** IRQEnable at `0x188` also accepts width 8. Consequently, “five readable registers × the site’s domain” overclaims X9/X10. Their extra width-8 cells cover only `0x180/0x280`.

The Clear drive partition has **five** operand cases: both zero; status zero only; enable zero only; both nonzero and disjoint; intersecting. The seven source lines are `{4,5,6,7,10,11,18}`, a union across the two machine configurations, not seven sources wired on each machine.

These are the **19 X14 rows**, with a distinguishing edit and the observable each actually holds. References to mutant IDs below are supported by the stored worker results; the [row implementation starts here](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/_scratchpad/fbd5/q2e/frozen/diff_footbridge.c:1353).

| Row | Cell | Distinguishing edit | Observable difference |
|---:|---|---|---|
| 1 | Disjoint Clear | N_S01, `&` → `&&` | CPU assert instead of deassert |
| 2 | Status-zero Clear | N_S02, test enable alone | CPU assert instead of deassert |
| 3 | Line 4 alone | Remove bit 4 from drive test | Deassert instead of assert |
| 4 | Line 5 alone | N_SB05 | Deassert instead of assert |
| 5 | Line 6 alone | N_SB06 | Deassert instead of assert |
| 6 | Line 7 alone | N_SB07 | Deassert instead of assert |
| 7 | Line 10 alone | N_SB10 / N_S06 | Deassert instead of assert |
| 8 | Line 11 alone | N_SB11 | Deassert instead of assert |
| 9 | Line 18 alone | N_SB18 | Deassert instead of assert |
| 10 | DEVINIT, five reads | N_T04/N_T05 | Expected read value becomes zero; announcement appears |
| 11 | DEVINIT, Set/Clear | N_F09 | Final enable becomes `0x30`, not `0x60` |
| 12 | DEVINIT, declines | F1 / N_T09 | Four announcements instead of five |
| 13 | DEVINIT + pending tick, reads | N_T04/N_T05 | Read value/announcement changes |
| 14 | DEVINIT + pending tick, Set/Clear | N_T06 / N_F15 | Enable becomes `0x70` / `0x30`, not `0x60` |
| 15 | DEVINIT + pending tick, declines | F1 / N_T09 | Four announcements instead of five |
| 16 | Producer-raised line 4, status read | N_T11 | Zero instead of `0x10`, plus announcement |
| 17 | Producer-raised line 4, Clear `0x10` | N_I02 | Required CPU deassert disappears |
| 18 | Producer-raised line 4, Clear `0x400` | N_I01 | Deassert replaces required assert |
| 19 | Producer-raised line 4, re-arm sequence | N_R05 / N_T10 | Final count becomes six / initial count becomes four |

None of these rows is wholly vacuous against its named edit. **That does not establish all the observables claimed for its cell.** Rows 10–15 never check interrupt counters. Row 16 checks counters during setup, then resets them and does not check them after the read. Row 19 checks neither interrupt calls nor legal-read values nor field preservation.

The exclusions for arbitrary exact values, extra joints, other-arm widths, other declined-write data, address aliases, Set’s drive test, other registers, and additional instance cells can stand under the stipulated floor. Undriven bits are unreachable in the examined machine wiring; actual interrupt level is explicitly excluded. **Those exclusions do not cover missing observations inside already rowed cells**, demonstrated next.

**2. Q2 — Three surviving edits, including a one-character minimum.**

These are **static predictions, not newly executed mutants**. All 862 rows are predicted green for each.

**A. One-character producer edit:** at `footbridge_interrupt_assert()`, change:

```c
d->irq_status |= interrupt->line;
```

to:

```c
d->irq_status ^= interrupt->line;
```

The detector makes exactly four real assert-callback invocations: one for the status-read instance, two for the Clear instances, one for the re-arm instance. Every invocation starts with `irq_status = 0`, `irq_enable = 0x10`, and line 4. OR and XOR therefore produce identical results on every invocation.

A repeated assertion would distinguish them: OR preserves the pending bit; XOR removes it. Producer repetition is not disclosed as excluded. The real deassert callback is never invoked either; all timer callbacks remain stubs.

**B. A condition inside a claimed X14 instance cell:** change Clear’s drive predicate to:

```c
if ((d->irq_status & d->irq_enable) || d->timer_control[0])
```

Every older #545 instance has `timer_control[0] == 0`. X14’s DEVINIT instances have `0x40`, making their Clear assert when its operands are disjoint. Their Set/Clear rows check return values, enable, and announcements—**no interrupt counter**. Later rows do not recover this observation.

This is precisely a drive-test condition on a timer field **at the DEVINIT values X14 claims to cover**, not a condition beyond X14’s cells.

**C. Wrong interrupt destination at covered narrow widths:** replace Clear’s assert call with:

```c
INTERRUPT_ASSERT(*(len < 4 ? &d->timer_irq[0] : &d->irq));
```

X9/X10/X12/X13 count the same stub regardless of destination. X14 always uses width 4 and therefore sees the correct destination. Narrow Clear writes can drive a timer interrupt while every claimed “clears and drives” row remains green.

The [narrow-write assertions](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/_scratchpad/fbd5/q2e/frozen/diff_footbridge.c:1269) expose the problem directly: they compare aggregate counters, not CPU-counter deltas.

The outstanding measurements are isolated detector runs for A–C. They would test producer history, missing X14 call assertions, and callback identity across existing widths.

**3. Q3 — Dispositions.**

| Finding | Judgment | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| G1 | **UPHOLD** | The added width-8 cells match the two edited aligned sites. |
| G2 | **REFINE** | The additions are useful; “return value was never read” is false—707’s X6 already checked it. |
| G3 | **UPHOLD** | X13 observes the missing narrow and all-ones Clear writes with latches set. |
| G4 | **UPHOLD** | Re-declining both sites detects the stated cross-site latch clearing. |
| G5 | **UPHOLD** | X11 now exercises the stated joint in all four register states. |
| G6 | **UPHOLD** | The agreed case boundary permits disclosure; declines do not consume `idata`. |
| G7 | **REFINE** | Exact-data disclosure is permitted, but latched zero-data Clear calls already exist in X7; their **effect** lacks an oracle. |
| G8 | **REFINE** | No duplicate row is needed for ordinary tail omission; X6’s poisoned buffers detect it. Include serialization in THE SPACE nonetheless. |
| G9 | **REFINE** | The fork and sampled-access corrections help; the replacement truncation claim remains overbroad. |
| G10 | **UPHOLD** | Explicitly naming the drive partition corrects the earlier omission. |
| O1 | **UPHOLD** | All 28 named escapes are recorded FAIL; I independently checked those 28 worker records. |
| O2 | **REFINE** | Reject the six unaligned rows; the aligned `0x188` read is valid and is **not** duplicated by repair 3. |
| O3 | **REFINE** | Partial re-seating is legitimate, but its implemented observations do not close its claimed cells; see Q2-B. |
| O4 | **REFINE** | Broadening the exclusion is sound only outside covered cells; its surviving-F1 example is stale. |
| O5 | **OVERTURN as class closure** | The unconditional wrong-target mutants die, but callback identity remains invisible at covered narrow widths; see Q2-C. |
| O6 | **REFINE** | Disclosure is allowed by the agreed floor; the stated enumeration of existing Set operand cases is false. |
| O7 | **UPHOLD** | The re-arm row detects both N_R05 and N_T10 in the stored results. |
| D1 | **UPHOLD** | Repair 2’s rejection is supported by the recorded counterexamples. |

**The accepted resolution that is wrong as a class closure is O5.** O3 also needs correction within the cells it chose to add; replacing every old instance is not the necessary remedy. O2’s alignment decision, O6’s scope decision, and G7/G8’s refusal to add redundant or out-of-floor rows are not themselves grounds for rejection.

For O2, enumeration settles the count: unaligned Z6 rows are reads at `184/284`, the Clear write at `18c`, and declines at `184/18c/284`: **six**. The five aligned rows are legal reads at `180/280/188` and declined writes at `180/280`. Only four are duplicated. The brief’s earlier “five unaligned” statement is wrong.

**4. Q4 — Records and counts.**

The changed comments still contain false or overbroad statements:

- **“Only writer” is false.** The helper comment, CONTRACT, and X14 header credit only `_assert`; `_deassert` also writes both fields.
- **“Never … DISJOINT” is false as an execution claim.** P8 already executes Clear with status `0x18`, new enable `0x20`. It omits the drive assertion. The missing axis there was observation, not reachability.
- **“No tick was ever pending” is false without a #545-only qualifier.** Earlier timer rows explicitly seed pending ticks. Scope the instance descriptions to the relevant P/X fixtures.
- **The Set operand census is wrong.** X9’s all-zero state plus Set `0x70` executes `(status,new_enable) = (0,0x70)`. `&&` still survives, but not for the stated “nonzero or both zero” enumeration.
- **F1 no longer “passes every row here.”** It fails both X14 DEVINIT decline rows. The [stored table](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/artifacts/fbd5/q2_20260923/r3_20260923/TABLE.txt:17) confirms this. Exact-value examples also need site/version qualification: the IRQ_STATUS and Clear direction variants using `irq_enable != 0x10` now fail.
- **The CONTRACT misattributes width-4/data-`0x70` Clear coverage.** X12/X13 contain five cells each per state; those width-4 cells belong to X9/X10.
- **The blanket COVERED introduction obscures which channels are observed.** X7 observes return/announcements; X14 has the omissions above. State the channels per row family.
- **The new truncation sentence is still false universally.** Cutting the recorded log after `862 rows, 0 failures` preserves both numeric checks and fails only the verdict check. The [gate greps](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/_scratchpad/fbd5/q2e/frozen/gate_offline.sh:572) search the entire file, not exclusively its last lines.

The arithmetic and pins themselves are correct:

|---|---|---:|

Repair delta:

`707 + 32 = 739; +8 = 747; +8 = 755; +8 = 763; +60 = 823; +20 = 843; +19 = 862.`

Whole-file census:

`32 timer rows + 78 P + 440 X1–X8 + 100 + 72 + 80 + 20 + 20 + 19 + 1 IDENTITY = 862.`

I independently counted the stored FIX log: every pin matches, with no duplicate P/X row names. I also compared all **652** worker records with the predictions and prior failing-name sets: **0 outcome mismatches, 0 variants losing a previously failing row**. These results support preservation of the measured kills, not closure of the remaining space.

**5. Q5 — There are additional dispositions.**

For **O3**, use an additive fixture matrix with shared assertions: retain synthetic states, add selected DEVINIT and producer-history states, and apply the same relevant return/value/state/latch/callback checks to each. A smaller immediate repair is to complete X14’s missing observations and give every existing Clear fixture separate CPU/timer counters. Neither requires replacing all 843 other rows. If producer behavior is intentionally outside scope, disclose that explicitly instead of implying that invoking it establishes coverage.

For **O6**, retain the scope exclusion while adding a separate regression item, or parameterize the operand/destination tests over Set and Clear with operation-specific setup. That supplies sibling protection without redefining the #545 floor.

Before shipping, measure the three Q2 witnesses and either repair their missing observations or narrow the contract where the agreed floor permits it.

| 9 | Line 18 alone | N_SB18 | Deassert instead of assert |
| 10 | DEVINIT, five reads | N_T04/N_T05 | Expected read value becomes zero; announcement appears |
| 11 | DEVINIT, Set/Clear | N_F09 | Final enable becomes `0x30`, not `0x60` |
| 12 | DEVINIT, declines | F1 / N_T09 | Four announcements instead of five |
| 13 | DEVINIT + pending tick, reads | N_T04/N_T05 | Read value/announcement changes |
| 14 | DEVINIT + pending tick, Set/Clear | N_T06 / N_F15 | Enable becomes `0x70` / `0x30`, not `0x60` |
| 15 | DEVINIT + pending tick, declines | F1 / N_T09 | Four announcements instead of five |
| 16 | Producer-raised line 4, status read | N_T11 | Zero instead of `0x10`, plus announcement |
| 17 | Producer-raised line 4, Clear `0x10` | N_I02 | Required CPU deassert disappears |
| 18 | Producer-raised line 4, Clear `0x400` | N_I01 | Deassert replaces required assert |
| 19 | Producer-raised line 4, re-arm sequence | N_R05 / N_T10 | Final count becomes six / initial count becomes four |

None of these rows is wholly vacuous against its named edit. **That does not establish all the observables claimed for its cell.** Rows 10–15 never check interrupt counters. Row 16 checks counters during setup, then resets them and does not check them after the read. Row 19 checks neither interrupt calls nor legal-read values nor field preservation.

The exclusions for arbitrary exact values, extra joints, other-arm widths, other declined-write data, address aliases, Set’s drive test, other registers, and additional instance cells can stand under the stipulated floor. Undriven bits are unreachable in the examined machine wiring; actual interrupt level is explicitly excluded. **Those exclusions do not cover missing observations inside already rowed cells**, demonstrated next.

**2. Q2 — Three surviving edits, including a one-character minimum.**

These are **static predictions, not newly executed mutants**. All 862 rows are predicted green for each.

**A. One-character producer edit:** at `footbridge_interrupt_assert()`, change:

```c
d->irq_status |= interrupt->line;
```

to:

```c
d->irq_status ^= interrupt->line;
```

The detector makes exactly four real assert-callback invocations: one for the status-read instance, two for the Clear instances, one for the re-arm instance. Every invocation starts with `irq_status = 0`, `irq_enable = 0x10`, and line 4. OR and XOR therefore produce identical results on every invocation.

A repeated assertion would distinguish them: OR preserves the pending bit; XOR removes it. Producer repetition is not disclosed as excluded. The real deassert callback is never invoked either; all timer callbacks remain stubs.

**B. A condition inside a claimed X14 instance cell:** change Clear’s drive predicate to:

```c
if ((d->irq_status & d->irq_enable) || d->timer_control[0])
```

Every older #545 instance has `timer_control[0] == 0`. X14’s DEVINIT instances have `0x40`, making their Clear assert when its operands are disjoint. Their Set/Clear rows check return values, enable, and announcements—**no interrupt counter**. Later rows do not recover this observation.

This is precisely a drive-test condition on a timer field **at the DEVINIT values X14 claims to cover**, not a condition beyond X14’s cells.

**C. Wrong interrupt destination at covered narrow widths:** replace Clear’s assert call with:

```c
INTERRUPT_ASSERT(*(len < 4 ? &d->timer_irq[0] : &d->irq));
```

X9/X10/X12/X13 count the same stub regardless of destination. X14 always uses width 4 and therefore sees the correct destination. Narrow Clear writes can drive a timer interrupt while every claimed “clears and drives” row remains green.

The [narrow-write assertions](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/_scratchpad/fbd5/q2e/frozen/diff_footbridge.c:1269) expose the problem directly: they compare aggregate counters, not CPU-counter deltas.

The outstanding measurements are isolated detector runs for A–C. They would test producer history, missing X14 call assertions, and callback identity across existing widths.

| G2 | **REFINE** | The additions are useful; “return value was never read” is false—707’s X6 already checked it. |

| G4 | **UPHOLD** | Re-declining both sites detects the stated cross-site latch clearing. |
| G5 | **UPHOLD** | X11 now exercises the stated joint in all four register states. |
| G6 | **UPHOLD** | The agreed case boundary permits disclosure; declines do not consume `idata`. |
| G7 | **REFINE** | Exact-data disclosure is permitted, but latched zero-data Clear calls already exist in X7; their **effect** lacks an oracle. |
| G8 | **REFINE** | No duplicate row is needed for ordinary tail omission; X6’s poisoned buffers detect it. Include serialization in THE SPACE nonetheless. |
| G9 | **REFINE** | The fork and sampled-access corrections help; the replacement truncation claim remains overbroad. |
| G10 | **UPHOLD** | Explicitly naming the drive partition corrects the earlier omission. |

| O4 | **REFINE** | Broadening the exclusion is sound only outside covered cells; its surviving-F1 example is stale. |
| O5 | **OVERTURN as class closure** | The unconditional wrong-target mutants die, but callback identity remains invisible at covered narrow widths; see Q2-C. |

| O7 | **UPHOLD** | The re-arm row detects both N_R05 and N_T10 in the stored results. |
| D1 | **UPHOLD** | Repair 2’s rejection is supported by the recorded counterexamples. |

**The accepted resolution that is wrong as a class closure is O5.** O3 also needs correction within the cells it chose to add; replacing every old instance is not the necessary remedy. O2’s alignment decision, O6’s scope decision, and G7/G8’s refusal to add redundant or out-of-floor rows are not themselves grounds for rejection.

For O2, enumeration settles the count: unaligned Z6 rows are reads at `184/284`, the Clear write at `18c`, and declines at `184/18c/284`: **six**. The five aligned rows are legal reads at `180/280/188` and declined writes at `180/280`. Only four are duplicated. The brief’s earlier “five unaligned” statement is wrong.

**4. Q4 — Records and counts.**

The changed comments still contain false or overbroad statements:

- **“Only writer” is false.** The helper comment, CONTRACT, and X14 header credit only `_assert`; `_deassert` also writes both fields.
- **“Never … DISJOINT” is false as an execution claim.** P8 already executes Clear with status `0x18`, new enable `0x20`. It omits the drive assertion. The missing axis there was observation, not reachability.
- **“No tick was ever pending” is false without a #545-only qualifier.** Earlier timer rows explicitly seed pending ticks. Scope the instance descriptions to the relevant P/X fixtures.
- **The Set operand census is wrong.** X9’s all-zero state plus Set `0x70` executes `(status,new_enable) = (0,0x70)`. `&&` still survives, but not for the stated “nonzero or both zero” enumeration.
- **F1 no longer “passes every row here.”** It fails both X14 DEVINIT decline rows. The [stored table](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/artifacts/fbd5/q2_20260923/r3_20260923/TABLE.txt:17) confirms this. Exact-value examples also need site/version qualification: the IRQ_STATUS and Clear direction variants using `irq_enable != 0x10` now fail.
- **The CONTRACT misattributes width-4/data-`0x70` Clear coverage.** X12/X13 contain five cells each per state; those width-4 cells belong to X9/X10.
- **The blanket COVERED introduction obscures which channels are observed.** X7 observes return/announcements; X14 has the omissions above. State the channels per row family.
- **The new truncation sentence is still false universally.** Cutting the recorded log after `862 rows, 0 failures` preserves both numeric checks and fails only the verdict check. The [gate greps](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/_scratchpad/fbd5/q2e/frozen/gate_offline.sh:572) search the entire file, not exclusively its last lines.

The arithmetic and pins themselves are correct:

|---|---|---:|

| X1–X8 | `12+11+3+10+2+248+148+6` | 440 |

Repair delta:

`707 + 32 = 739; +8 = 747; +8 = 755; +8 = 763; +60 = 823; +20 = 843; +19 = 862.`

Whole-file census:

`32 timer rows + 78 P + 440 X1–X8 + 100 + 72 + 80 + 20 + 20 + 19 + 1 IDENTITY = 862.`

I independently counted the stored FIX log: every pin matches, with no duplicate P/X row names. I also compared all **652** worker records with the predictions and prior failing-name sets: **0 outcome mismatches, 0 variants losing a previously failing row**. These results support preservation of the measured kills, not closure of the remaining space.

**5. Q5 — There are additional dispositions.**

For **O3**, use an additive fixture matrix with shared assertions: retain synthetic states, add selected DEVINIT and producer-history states, and apply the same relevant return/value/state/latch/callback checks to each. A smaller immediate repair is to complete X14’s missing observations and give every existing Clear fixture separate CPU/timer counters. Neither requires replacing all 843 other rows. If producer behavior is intentionally outside scope, disclose that explicitly instead of implying that invoking it establishes coverage.

For **O6**, retain the scope exclusion while adding a separate regression item, or parameterize the operand/destination tests over Set and Clear with operation-specific setup. That supplies sibling protection without redefining the #545 floor.

Before shipping, measure the three Q2 witnesses and either repair their missing observations or narrow the contract where the agreed floor permits it.
